  without satisfying all five locally; “selling something other than a universal quantum computer” is fair only
  after specifying that target.
- **Criterion 1:** Useful computational subsystems need not be literal two-level physical systems; leakage-managed
  multilevel encodings are common.
- **Criterion 3:** $T_\text{coh}/T_\text{gate}$ alone is not a reliable gate budget under stochastic gate error,
  crosstalk, leakage, idling, and QEC cycles.
- **Criterion 4:** Universality can be achieved through gates plus measurement/feed-forward; not every architecture
  needs arbitrary unitary approximation in the simple native-gate sense.
- **Criterion 5:** Individual qubit projective measurement is stronger than some universal schemes require.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.14 superconducting platform bullet

- **Perishability:** All named generations, coherence/error figures, and “most on one chip” claims require a dated
  warning and current sources.
- **Technical defect:** Cross-resonance is not simply a “natural” two-qubit gate; it is a driven interaction
  engineered from fixed coupling and compiled into echoed gates.
- **Taxonomy:** Condor's large qubit count did not imply the leading useful processor; naming it beside Heron
  without metric context repeats the count trap warned against later.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 trapped-ion platform bullet

- **Technical defect:** Not all listed species encode qubits in hyperfine ground-state sublevels—`40Ca+` has no
  nuclear spin and commonly uses optical qubits.
- **Connectivity qualification:** “All-to-all” is mediated and performance degrades with chain size/mode
  complexity; it is not cost-free graph connectivity.
- **Current superlative:** “Highest demonstrated” requires metric, date, and comparable gate definition.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 neutral-atom platform bullet

- **Current claim ambiguity:** “Thousands of qubits per device demonstrated” can mean trapped/arranged atoms, not
  thousands of high-fidelity programmable logical gate qubits. Name the demonstrated operation.
- **Technical nuance:** Rydberg interactions include van der Waals and dipole–dipole regimes; the simple shift
  description is adequate but not universal.
- **Connectivity:** Physical rearrangement changes geometry between circuit stages and has latency/loss; it does
  not imply arbitrary instantaneous connectivity.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 photonics platform bullet

- **Factual defect:** Photons do not travel through fiber “with little loss” over long distances; fiber attenuation
  is the central networking limitation and motivates repeaters.
- **Taxonomy:** Xanadu's continuous-variable Gaussian boson sampling is not naturally a dual-rail/polarization
  qubit implementation; separate discrete-variable and continuous-variable photonics.
- **KLM nuance:** Gates are heralded/probabilistic at primitive level, while fault-tolerant resource architectures
  can achieve effectively scalable computation with overhead.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 silicon-spin and defect-centre bullets

- **Silicon manufacturing overclaim:** Existing CMOS infrastructure cannot simply be “retooled”; cryogenic
  operation, isotopic purification, variability, and quantum-grade control impose major process differences.
- **Defect-centre coherence:** Milliseconds/seconds depend on nuclear bath, dynamical decoupling, temperature, and
  whether electron or nuclear memory is measured. Date/source the range.
- **Platform framing:** Calling defects “primarily networking” underweights sensing, which the same sentence later
  acknowledges.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §0.14 topological-qubit bullet

- **Major conceptual defect:** Majorana zero modes are not themselves non-Abelian anyons in generic nanowire
  evidence, and braiding Majorana modes alone is not computationally universal without supplementary operations.
- **Currency:** June 2, 2026 claims are extremely fresh relative to this July 2026 review; they require primary
  source verification, exact preprint identifiers, and careful separation of company claim from replication.
- **Editorial concern:** Naming one critic (“Henry Legg and others”) without citations reads like argument by
  selected authority. Cite the specific technical objection and response.
- **Potential false precision:** “One of two measurement types required” needs definition and source.
- **Balance:** This paragraph is far longer than every established platform bullet; justified by controversy, but
  it distorts platform weight. Move the evolving case study to Appendix F/Chapter 20.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.14 platform comparison table

- **Count mismatch:** Prose says “same six gate-model platforms,” table has six, but topological is excluded without
  explicit explanation and photonics includes non-gate examples elsewhere.
- **Metric comparability:** “Highest fidelity,” qubit counts, and coherence compare different gates, duty cycles,
  encodings, and measurement conventions.
- **Accessibility:** Dense qualitative cells need sources/date and perhaps symbols separating established,
  demonstrated, and roadmap properties.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 bargains-with-nature synthesis

- **Positive:** Excellent memorable comparative framing.
- **Logical defect:** Diversity is not necessarily “a sign of maturity”; it can equally indicate immaturity and
  absence of a converged architecture. Say it is compatible with both healthy exploration and nonconvergence.
- **Defect-centre judgment:** “Poor fit” is overly categorical given modular/networked architectures.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.14 qubit-count warning

- **Positive:** One of the strongest engineering passages.
- **Overstatement:** The million-versus-thousand comparison cannot conclude which is “smaller” without logical
  circuit, code threshold, cycle time, decoder, topology/routing, and correlated noise; the chosen raw error rates
  alone may put one above threshold, but universal ordering is unjustified.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.14 quantum system versus useful computer

- **Positive:** Essential demarcation and well phrased.
- **Minor style:** “Almost always quantum” is technically true of matter but rhetorically weak; the relevant issue
  is whether coherent quantum degrees of freedom are controlled as computational resources.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 adiabatic, annealing, D-Wave

- **Technical qualification:** Adiabatic/gate polynomial equivalence assumes idealized models, spectral gaps,
  precision, and appropriate Hamiltonians; “state is kept” should be “ideally remains approximately.”
- **Taxonomy:** Annealing hardware may be universal under augmented controls in principle, but commercial D-Wave
  operation is correctly distinguished from universal gate computation.
- **Marketing claim:** “Commercial from late 2000s” and current thousands count are perishable.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 measurement-based computation and close

- **Technical defect:** MBQC is not “non-gate-model” in the same sense as annealing; it is a universal circuit-
  equivalent model, and cluster-state preparation itself commonly uses entangling gates/fusions.
- **Photonics overgeneralization:** Measurement is not simply cheap; high-efficiency number-resolving detection,
  feed-forward latency, source loss, and fusion success are core difficulties.
- **Positive:** The final list of noise/control/fabrication/calibration/software effectively pivots to engineering.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.15 NISQ framing

- **Definition drift:** NISQ does not have a stable hard range “tens to thousands”; use qualitative scale and date.
- **Error-rate range:** $10^{-4}$ physical gate error at meaningful scale is an exceptional/best-case claim and
  should distinguish one- versus two-qubit gates.
- **Historical causality:** VQE predates Preskill's 2018 label; Qiskit/Cirq also did not all “develop around” that
  framing. Say the category organized/accelerated an existing ecosystem.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.15 advantage claims

- **Historical/current defect:** IBM's “2.5 days” was a projection under assumptions, not necessarily a completed
  corresponding computation, while later classical simulations used varied fidelity/task definitions.
- **Epistemic overreach:** “The claim was real” conflicts with the subsequent baseline dispute. Say the experiment
  and sampling achievement were real; magnitude/classical separation remained contested.
- **Current-status defect:** “Above reach of algorithms published when performed” is a weak retrospective
  definition of advantage and may be false for exact task/fidelity comparisons. Use best demonstrated classical
  baseline at the time and cite.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.15 Willow

- **Overclaim:** “First experimental confirmation in print that the surface-code threshold can be reached” depends
  on definitions and prior repetition-code/surface-code experiments. State the precise repeated-round distance-
  scaling result and comparator.
- **Terminology:** Below-threshold scaling of a memory is not yet a general fault-tolerant logical gate stack.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.15 Quantinuum/Microsoft

- **Likely category error:** “Logical error rates 800× lower than underlying physical error rates” requires
  like-for-like operation/benchmark definitions; ratios across different tasks can mislead.
- **Strong current-claim concern:** “48 fully error-corrected logical qubits” at 2:1 encoding is company
  terminology that may mean error-detected/logically encoded qubits, not general fault-tolerant qubits. The prose
  flags company reporting but still repeats “fully error-corrected” too readily.
- **SPAM mismatch:** 99.99% SPAM fidelity does not establish logical-gate or QEC quality.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.15 Atom/Microsoft and cross-platform synthesis

- **Terminology defect:** Entangled encoded/logical qubits and “useful fault-tolerance” are not synonymous with
  below-threshold scalable QEC.
- **Record claim:** “Largest ... then on record” needs a precisely defined category and independent source.
- **Overclaim:** Calling all three “first credible footprint of useful fault-tolerance” bundles incomparable
  demonstrations and is editorially promotional. Say they demonstrate complementary pieces.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.15 present snapshot

- **Date issue:** The review date is July 19, 2026; a June 2026 snapshot must now be actively rechecked, especially
  software stacks, Majorana 2, standards, and Helios claims.
- **Overstatement:** Cloud access has made experiments “routine” only subject to access, queues, cost, region, and
  vendor availability.
- **Unsupported forecast:** Roadmaps targeting hundreds of logical qubits and early-2030s/2040s cryptanalytic
  ranges require named surveys and distributions; otherwise they launder speculation into a textbook timeline.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.15 final calibration

- **Positive:** Concise, honest, and an excellent emotional stance for the reader.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.16 arc summary

- **Positive:** Impressive compression with strong causal continuity.
- **Overclaim:** “Below-threshold footprints on multiple platforms” repeats the cross-platform category problem;
  only Willow is earlier called strict below-threshold.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.16 overturned-assumptions table

- **Conceptual defects:** Classical energy is not universally continuous; light is not replaced by “also photons”
  without field quantization nuance; Born's rule did not force the full state-vector ontology; measurements are not
  universally projections; information physicality is broader than Landauer alone; Feynman did not prove quantum
  machines are demanded; QEC does not show generic fragile states can always be protected under arbitrary noise.
- **Improvement:** Rename columns “Useful classical default,” “Quantum-theory replacement/extension,” and “Key
  pressure or result.” This preserves the teaching device without presenting a simplistic sequence of decisive
  single experiments.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.16 engineering-order paragraph

- **Ordering defect:** It says learn operations Chapters 6–11, then constraints 17–19, then algorithms 13–16,
  which is not actual reading order. If describing conceptual order, say so; otherwise put algorithms before
  constraints.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.16 carry-forward bullets

- **Overstatement:** Quantum postulates are not simply empirical survivors from 1900–1927; they are idealized
  mathematical codifications refined later.
- **Major conceptual defect:** “Entanglement ... behind ... most algorithmic speedups” is not generally established;
  some speedups can occur with limited entanglement and resource requirements depend on model. Avoid naming it the
  central resource for all computation without nuance.
- **Uncomputation history:** Bennett's reversible simulation inspires the pattern, but “exactly” is stronger than
  warranted.
- **Grover framing:** Saying it is “what you get when no structure is available” is useful but only in the oracle
  model.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.16 closing checklist

- **Pedagogy:** Excellent retrieval practice without answers.
- **Conceptual caution:** Tensor products are mathematical composition rules not forced by entanglement alone;
  quantum speedups do not all reduce to a single interference slogan.
- **Formatting:** A list inside a blockquote may render inconsistently across pipelines; existing lint passes, but
  visually inspect it.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.16 interpretation boundary and final paragraphs

- **Positive:** Explicitly separates operational predictions from metaphysical commitment.
- **Potential overstatement:** Interpretations are empirically equivalent in their standard forms, but hardware
  and algorithms are not literally “interpretation-agnostic” if one considers collapse modifications or
  interpretation-inspired extensions. “Do not require choosing among empirically equivalent standard
  interpretations” is exact.
- **Wording:** Equating collapse shorthand solely with Born rule plus projective measurement omits state-update
  conditioning.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The last “either path” resolution closes both the historical story and navigation loop.

#### Three-track timeline — framing

- **Positive:** Reading down versus across gives the table a real interpretive instruction.
- **Conceptual concern:** The tracks were never independent—physics depended on instrumentation/engineering,
  information theory emerged from communications engineering, and computation depended on logic plus hardware.
  Use “partly distinct” rather than “independently developing.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Three-track timeline — 1859–1900 row

- **Precision:** “Maxwellian confidence” is mood rather than an event and does not parallel the concrete items in
  other cells. “Maxwellian electromagnetic synthesis and experimental confirmation” is more informative.
- **Omission suspicion:** Thermodynamics/statistical mechanics are central to Episode I yet absent.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### Three-track timeline — 1900–1925 row

- **Category issue:** Rutherford and Stern–Gerlach are experimental engineering/instrumentation as well as
  physics; the rigid columns obscure cross-feeding.
- **Overstatement:** “Formal computation not yet established” is fair, but foundational logic and mechanical
  computation were advancing; the prior row already says early formal logic.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### Three-track timeline — 1925–1935 row

- **Chronology:** Von Neumann (1932), EPR/Schrödinger (1935) fit; Dirac's bra–ket contribution does not, though his
  foundational work does.
- **Vagueness:** “Quantum theory begins informing materials” needs an example or should move band theory/Bloch
  explicitly here.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### Three-track timeline — 1936–1964 row

- **Ordering defect:** Bell's theorem is at the *end* of a 28-year span and visually precedes much earlier QFT/
  solid-state work. Break the row or order chronologically.
- **Category duplication:** Superconductivity was discovered in 1911 but its theory/engineering spans rows; label
  BCS/Josephson rather than generic superconductivity.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Three-track timeline — 1970s–1980s row

- **Chronology defect:** Wiesner's idea dates to the late 1960s though publication is 1983; the table silently picks
  publication chronology.
- **Category concern:** Benioff/Feynman/Deutsch belong as much under physics/computation convergence as pure
  “computation & information,” which is the intended diagonal but needs explanation.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Three-track timeline — 1990s row

- **Overstatement:** “Entanglement becomes an operational resource” began with E91/dense coding/teleportation, but
  the row should name them rather than leaving the physics column abstract.
- **QEC category:** Error correction is both computation/information and engineering architecture.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Three-track timeline — 2000s–present row and coda

- **Range defect:** “2000s–present” collapses more than 25 years and mixes loophole-free tests (2015), NISQ (2018),
  modern neutral-atom scaling, and earlier platform development. Split 2000s/2010s/2020s.
- **Taxonomy:** Quantum simulation appears under physics despite being a computational algorithm/application too.
- **Rhetoric:** The “bottom-left-to-bottom-right diagonal” is not visually well defined in a three-column table
  with multi-item rows. “Bottom rows across all three columns” is clearer.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Source notes — citation strategy

- **Major concern:** The claim that historical assertions “are not in serious dispute” is contradicted by the
  chapter's many priority, motivation, quotation, interpretation, and recent-industry claims. Narrative form does
  not remove the need for localized citations.
- **Improvement:** Add compact numbered endnotes or per-paragraph citation markers. A single grouped bibliography
  cannot tell which source supports “first,” “highest,” reported probabilities, anecdotes, or quotations.
- **Reader value:** Sources do help, rather than slow, readers who want to challenge claims—the text itself says
  this. The stated anti-citation rationale is internally inconsistent.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### Source notes — Episode I

- **Coverage gaps:** No source is supplied for the unattributed 1894 “decimal places” claim, Stefan–Boltzmann/Wien/
  Rayleigh–Jeans chronology, atomic-collapse timescale, Balmer/Rydberg formulas, Planck's “purely formal” wording,
  Nobel-committee inference, Sommerfeld, Stern–Gerlach, Compton, de Broglie, Pauli, or old-theory limitations.
- **Bibliographic precision:** Article titles/DOIs would make old journal volume/page citations discoverable.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### Source notes — Episode II

- **Coverage gaps:** No Bohm 1951/1952 references, Solvay source, Tsirelson source, Garg–Mermin/Eberhard thresholds,
  Rowe/Giustina/Christensen 2001/2013 sources, cosmic Bell sources, or E91 citation.
- **Major mismatch:** These omissions correspond exactly to the most delicate claims and quantitative values.
- **Positive:** Primary sources for core formalism and 2015 tests are present.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### Source notes — Episode III

- **Coverage gaps:** No laser/maser, NMR, integrated-circuit, DiVincenzo-era engineering, first BB84 prototype,
  Micius, twin-field QKD, or PQC/QKD deployment sources.
- **Historical ambiguity:** Citing Bardeen–Brattain and Shockley's later book does not directly support every
  priority/date/manufacturability claim in the transistor paragraph.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### Source notes — Episode IV foundations and algorithms

- **Coverage gaps:** Manin, Deutsch 1989 networks, Yao 1993 equivalence, Deutsch–Jozsa, Bernstein–Vazirani,
  amplitude amplification, phase estimation lineage, HHL, QAOA/VQE, and Simon rejection anecdote are missing.
- **Bibliographic ambiguity:** “Simon's algorithm is in the same proceedings” is not a complete citation.
- **Positive:** The canonical Shor/Grover/QEC/threshold works are at least identified.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### Source notes — Episode IV hardware/current claims

- **Critical gap:** Almost none of §0.14's platform metrics, companies, Majorana 1/2 claims/criticism, Sycamore
  classical comparisons, Quantinuum/Atom records, or roadmap forecasts appears in the notes.
- **Currency defect:** Majorana 2 and Helios are newer than several source-note entries and require exact primary
  links plus access dates.
- **Willow:** One paper cannot support all cross-platform “below threshold/useful fault tolerance” synthesis.
- **NIST:** Press releases are acceptable for selection dates, but standards pages/FIPS documents should support
  formal status.
- **Entertainment: 1/5.** The missing provenance actively weakens confidence in the chapter's most exciting claims.

#### Recommended source-note redesign

- Attach a stable citation key to every paragraph containing a quotation, priority claim, superlative, exact date,
  experimental number, current vendor claim, or historical motivation.
- Separate primary evidence from historical interpretation and company-reported material.
- Give current claims an “as verified on YYYY-MM-DD” ledger entry and avoid unsupported “first/highest/largest.”
- Retain the readable narrative by using unobtrusive endnote markers rather than parenthetical citation clusters.
- **Entertainment impact: 4/5 expected.** Trustworthy provenance lets curious readers follow the story without
  making the prose feel defensive.

#### Footer navigation

- **No defect found:** Previous/TOC/next links are correct and separated by the required rule.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### Historical Prelude — second-pass file verdict

- **Overall:** Exceptionally engaging and structurally ambitious, but its confident narrative voice repeatedly
  outruns its qualifications. The strongest revision need is not stylistic; it is epistemic precision around
  classical assumptions, historical priority/motivation, Bell-test statistics, algorithmic complexity conditions,
  QEC guarantees, platform comparisons, and very recent company claims.
- **Strong-defect shortlist:** classical separability/commutativity definitions; anomaly count/cross-reference;
  hydrogen continuum omission; EPR/classical-correlation overclaim; Bell p-value interpretation; BB84 incorrectly
  listed as an entanglement protocol; “sub-millikelvin” superconducting control; Church–Turing characterization;
  Shannon coding interpretation; zero thermodynamic cost claim; Shor discrete log in “arbitrary groups”; CSS
  containment statement; `40Ca+` hyperfine claim; low-loss fiber claim; cross-platform below-threshold synthesis;
  and missing sources for most delicate assertions.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Even where it needs correction, this is compelling history. Preserve the causal arc,
  debugging metaphors, controlled antitheses, and anti-hype checkpoints while tightening claims.
- **Second-pass status:** Complete for this file; all prose, timeline, source notes, and navigation were read in
  isolated section ranges before Chapter 1 was opened.

### `book/part-01-orientation/01-why-quantum-computing-exists.md` — second pass

#### File header, opening thesis, four-paper arc, and reading guide

- **Positive:** The first sentence is crisp, accurate at the intended level, and differentiates machine model from
  clock-speed marketing.
- **Technical qualification:** A general state can be mixed and described by a density operator, open evolution is
  nonunitary, and outputs need not be only final computational-basis samples. Since this is motivational, say the
  description is the ideal closed gate model.
- **Overpromise:** “Every subsequent chapter pays the gesture back” is absolute and probably false for every
  frontier/industry claim. “The foundational chapters” is safer.
- **Four-paper ambiguity:** The unnamed four are presumably Feynman, Deutsch, Shor, Grover, but the first is a
  published lecture and dates/history are richer. Name them once.
- **Major overclaim:** Feynman gave a structural reason general classical simulation *appears costly*; he did not
  prove the answer is no. The claim directly repeats a problem found in the Prelude.
- **Current overclaim:** Hardware is not clearly “demonstrating the first two regimes”: quantum simulation advantage
  of useful systems and universal-computer advantage need explicit experiments/tasks. Define the regimes.
- **Timeline:** “Years to decades” is speculative and should be attributed/date-stamped.
- **Reading-guide contradiction:** “There is no mathematics ... not seen by anyone who read a popular account” is
  implausible given BPP/BQP, complexity assumptions, QFT structure, and resource distinctions. It may alienate a
  reader whose popular source was lighter.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.1 Feynman setup

- **Overstatement:** Again, Feynman's answer is reported as “in general, no” rather than an argument/motivation.
  No unconditional lower bound rules out all efficient classical simulation formulations.
- **Example precision:** $n$ interacting electrons do not map directly to $n$ qubits without basis/discretization
  choices; “$n$ two-level spins or a discretized fermionic system” is safer.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.1 statevector scaling

- **Numerical defect:** $2^{50}$ complex amplitudes are about $1.126\times10^{15}$; at 16 bytes each they require
  roughly 18 PB, beyond ordinary aggregate RAM of many leading systems rather than merely “at the edge,” depending
  on date/system. State bytes and precision.
- **Popular-analogy concern:** The atoms-in-universe comparison for $2^{300}\approx2\times10^{90}$ is broadly true
  but contributes drama rather than computational insight.
- **Algorithm taxonomy:** DMRG is a tensor-network method, so listing tensor networks and DMRG as peers overlaps.
  Quantum Monte Carlo's success conditions include sign-problem structure, not generally “low temperature.”
- **Complexity claim:** “None can exist” needs a defined simulation task and assumptions such as BQP not contained
  in BPP; generic dynamics can mean many inequivalent input/output problems.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.1 quantum simulation motivation

- **Conceptual defect:** Equal Hilbert-space dimension does not by itself remove exponential overhead; efficient
  state preparation, Hamiltonian implementation, time, precision, and measurement remain.
- **Metaphor risk:** “Absorbs the exponential structurally” is memorable but can imply accessible exponential
  storage/parallelism. Say local quantum degrees of freedom realize the joint evolution without explicit
  classical amplitude storage.
- **Priority nuance:** Simulation was a foundational motivation, but “the original motivation” erases Manin,
  Benioff, and other antecedents named in the Prelude.
- **Forecast:** “Most likely near-term advantage” is a current expert judgment, not settled fact; cite a survey and
  define near term/practical.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.1 Deutsch

- **Comparison defect:** The paragraph says “classical query complexity” after specifying deterministic classical
  needs two. Bounded-error randomized comparison differs; keep “deterministic.”
- **Historical defect carried forward:** The original Deutsch algorithm was probabilistic/inconclusive as the
  Prelude itself carefully states, while this paragraph presents the modern clean one-query separation without
  qualification. Cross-file inconsistency.
- **Question mismatch:** Deutsch did not establish efficient function computation beyond probabilistic Turing
  machines in this tiny example.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.1 two structural facts and slogan

- **Technical overstatement:** Not all quantum operations are unitary; ideal closed evolution/gates are. The book
  later emphasizes measurement and channels.
- **Logical defect:** Exponential dimension alone is not “why classical simulation is hard”; structured states
  admit compact representations. The combination of reachable entanglement/dynamics/task matters.
- **Algorithmic overstatement:** Interference is necessary language for many algorithms but does not by itself
  explain all speedups or constitute a sufficient resource.
- **Cross-reference:** “Mechanics in Chapter 10” is odd because postulates/evolution arrive in Chapter 5 and qubits/
  gates in 6–9. Chapter 10 focuses phenomena.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.2 all-possibilities misconception

- **Positive:** Strong corrective opening.
- **Technical wording:** A circuit need not prepare a superposition over all $2^n$ basis states; it *can* prepare a
  full-support state. Replace “does prepare.”
- **Measurement wording:** General quantum algorithms may output several bits, repeated samples, expectation
  estimates, or quantum states—not always “a single bit string.”
- **Conceptual issue:** Calling component-wise unitary action “parallel evaluations” even in quotation marks risks
  preserving the bad computational accounting.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.2 interference paragraph

- **Overgeneralization:** Not every algorithm labels paths “correct/incorrect” or uses simple destructive/
  constructive amplification; phase estimation, sampling, simulation, and relational problems are subtler.
- **Positive:** Medium/algorithm/readout triad is memorable and useful if introduced as a design pattern, not a
  universal theorem.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.2 workload negatives and Grover ceiling

- **Unsupported broad claims:** There are quantum subroutines/proposals for linear algebra, image processing,
  databases, compilation optimization, and ML. The correct claim is no known *generic end-to-end practical
  speedup* for ordinary versions/input models.
- **False universal:** “When structure is absent, the best ... provably is Grover” holds in black-box unstructured
  search/query models, not every structureless task or computational model.
- **Overhead claim:** Error correction does not necessarily “eat” the speedup; oracle construction/data loading and
  quantum/classical clock differences may dominate. Say total overhead.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.2 Church–Turing and NP negatives

- **Precision:** A quantum computation is classically simulable in the computability sense under standard finite/
  computable amplitudes, but the Church–Turing thesis should not be summarized as a theorem about physical machines
  without nuance.
- **Extended-thesis wording defect:** It does not posit “classical and quantum computation are polynomially
  equivalent” historically; quantum is the candidate physical countermodel to efficient classical simulation.
- **Complexity nuance:** Grover is not “the best general result on NP”; it gives black-box brute-force speedups for
  search formulations, while quantum algorithms for particular NP problems may exploit structure.
- **Evidence wording:** Oracle results are relativized evidence and can point in different directions; absence of
  an algorithm is weak evidence. State consensus carefully.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.2 final definition

- **Model limitation:** This defines an ideal gate-model sampler, not quantum annealers, analog simulators,
  measurement-based systems, open-system computation, or quantum-output protocols.
- **Measurement defect:** Measurements are not limited to projection onto an orthonormal basis; Chapter 11 includes
  POVMs. The chapter's own broad definition should not regress to projective-only language.
- **Overstatement:** “Everything else is consequence” is elegant but excludes architecture assumptions, control,
  complexity definitions, and resource models not dictated solely by postulates.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.3 opening

- **Count defect:** “Four papers in the 1990s” is not followed by four paper-based categories: Shor, Grover,
  quantum simulation (many papers, including Feynman/Lloyd), and optimization/ML (many post-2014 programs).
  Rewrite the opening to match the actual taxonomy.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.3 Shor

- **Asymptotic precision:** GNFS complexity in terms of an $n$-bit integer is
  $\exp(O(n^{1/3}(\log n)^{2/3}))` up to constants if translating from $N`; specify whether logs and $n$ denote
  bit length. The formula is broadly shaped correctly but easy to misread.
- **Cryptographic scope:** Discrete logarithm algorithms apply in appropriate finite abelian groups; “the same
  algorithmic idea” is safer than “same algorithm,” but name that domain.
- **Overstatement:** RSA/DH/ECC are not the totality of “today's public-key cryptography,” especially after PQC
  standardization/deployment.
- **Causal speculation:** Cryptanalysis is *a major reason* for funding, not demonstrably “why the field has the
  funding profile.”
- **Historical status:** NIST's PQC effort is no longer simply an ongoing competition in 2026; standards have been
  finalized and additional algorithms remain in process.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.3 Grover

- **Major overclaim:** Not every predicate search automatically receives an end-to-end $\sqrt N$ speedup; the
  predicate must be implemented coherently, oracle costs counted, sufficient workspace available, and output
  verification/repetitions included.
- **SAT/graph claim:** Quadratic improvement over brute force is not necessarily a quadratic speedup over the best
  structured classical algorithm.
- **Overhead focus:** Blaming “every gate wrapped in error correction” is simplistic; fault-tolerant logical clock
  rates, oracle depth, memory, data access, and classical parallelism determine crossover.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.3 quantum simulation

- **Technical overstatement:** Polynomial guarantees require norms, sparsity/locality, oracle/access models,
  finite-dimensional encoding, simulation time, and chosen error metric. “Generic interacting systems cost
  exponential classically” remains conjectural/task-specific.
- **Forecast defect:** “Hundreds to thousands,” “moderate-depth,” and “within a decade” are unsupported and
  application-dependent; useful chemistry may need huge non-Clifford counts despite modest logical-qubit width.
- **Taxonomy:** QSP is a technique underpinning simulation transformations, not simply a peer algorithm family
  without explanation.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.3 optimization and ML

- **Positive:** Appropriately skeptical and clear about lack of demonstrated practical advantage.
- **Category issue:** Quantum annealing, QAOA, VQE, and QML solve different tasks under different models; grouping
  them as one “category” hides important evidence differences.
- **Dequantization overreach:** Tang-style recommendation dequantization did not generally reduce “linear-system
  solvers” to zero advantage; quantum-inspired algorithms revise particular data-access models and regimes.
- **“Honest” style:** Calling the book's own summary “honest” implicitly labels alternatives dishonest. Present
  evidence and let the calibration demonstrate honesty.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.3 speedup taxonomy — exponential

- **Major classification defect:** “All are believed, none unconditionally proven” conflates algorithmic upper
  bounds (proven) with separations from optimal classical algorithms (usually conjectural). State that quantum
  runtimes are proven under assumptions, while exponential *advantages* rely on classical hardness assumptions.
- **Simulation:** Generic local-Hamiltonian simulation quantum runtime need not imply exponential advantage for
  every observable/state/instance.
- **HHL:** Its complexity dependence includes condition number, precision, sparsity, preparation, and readout; the
  text does flag restrictive access but not output restrictions here.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.3 speedup taxonomy — polynomial

- **Potential technical defect:** Collision-finding complexities depend on problem variant/query model; the
  classical birthday bound is randomized, while element-distinctness input size notation should be consistent.
- **Graph claim:** “$\sqrt n$-style speedups” is too vague to review or learn from; name a problem and bound.
- **“Unconditionally provable”:** Query separations are provable only under formal oracle/promise models; say that.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.3 speculative coda

- **Contradiction:** “Advantage exists in specific narrowly scoped cases at most” can mean proven existence, while
  preceding text says no convincing practical demonstration. Replace with “evidence/candidate advantages are
  limited to...”.
- **Tone:** “Caveat emptor” is engaging but slightly glib after a nuanced taxonomy.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.4 opening negatives

- **Overclaim:** “Do not help” is much stronger than “no known generic practical speedup.” Quantum subroutines may
  aid specialized compilation, optimization, sampling, or ML formulations even if ordinary workloads remain
  classical.
- **Sorting defect:** Quantum comparison sorting has $\Omega(n\log n)$ comparisons, but time/parallel/query models
  require qualification; “below” is okay only for comparison model.
- **Multiplication claim:** “Below best known classical bound” is tautological temporally and could change; no
  lower bound establishes that quantum cannot improve integer multiplication.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.4 structural reason and million-qubit thought experiment

- **Conceptual overreach:** Practical workloads can contain algebraic/optimization/sampling subproblems even when
  the whole stack is I/O-bound. The right systems model is quantum acceleration, not replacing a web stack.
- **Thought-experiment weakness:** A million *noise-free* qubits could plausibly accelerate selected backend tasks;
  asking it to “run the web stack” constructs a category error and then rejects it.
- **Positive:** “Specific mathematical primitives, not computation in general” is the correct core.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.4 Grover lower bound

- **Positive:** Explicit black-box qualification is present here, unlike several earlier universalized versions.
- **Precision:** Optimality is $\Theta(\sqrt N)$ for bounded-error query complexity under a marked-item promise;
  “structure genuinely absent” is informal model interpretation.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.4 sorting

- **Likely defect:** “At most polylogarithmic improvements” is vague and may be false depending on comparison,
  query, depth, and non-comparison models. The key comparison-query count is still $\Theta(n\log n)$; state the
  exact model/result.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### §1.4 NP-complete problems

- **Overclaim:** “No known ... better than Grover-type quadratic over brute force” ignores problem-specific quantum
  algorithms with improvements measured against particular exact/exponential algorithms, and “brute force” is not
  the best classical baseline for many NP-complete problems.
- **Complexity belief:** NP not contained in BQP is the relevant broad belief, but individual NP-complete algorithm
  comparisons require care.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.4 HHL

- **Technical defect:** HHL is not simply polylogarithmic in all relevant parameters; it has polynomial dependence
  on condition number and inverse precision, plus sparsity/oracle costs.
- **Overstatement:** Removing one assumption does not necessarily make “the speedup vanish”; it can degrade or
  require different algorithms. State which assumptions are necessary for the headline exponential claim.
- **Positive:** Quantum-state output versus classical-vector output is the crucial caveat.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.4 low-exponent problems and evaluation lesson

- **Positive:** Wall-clock analysis warning is excellent.
- **Incomplete checklist:** Add output/readout, success probability, precision, hardware parallelism, data movement,
  and best classical baseline to structure/input access.
- **Overgeneralization:** “Most of the time” one question exposes a claim as narrow/illusory is unsupported and
  adversarially toned.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.5 terminology

- **Taxonomy concern:** Quantum advantage is not simply two meanings; literature distinguishes computational
  advantage, practical advantage, utility, quantum-centric supercomputing, and scientific value. Present these as
  the book's working distinction.
- **Term history:** “Largely retired” varies by community/geography; no need to assert a settled retirement.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.5 computational advantage

- **Definition problem:** “Faster than best known algorithm on best hardware” is practically unverifiable and
  changes; experiments usually compare against best implemented/estimated baselines at a defined fidelity.
- **Sycamore nuance:** Random-circuit sampling's verification, fidelity, sampling definition, and classical
  counterclaims need more than one sentence if used as canonical example.
- **Value claim:** Such tasks validate control/complexity assumptions, but “genuinely hard” remains contingent on
  classical algorithms and task definition.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.5 practical utility

- **Definition strength:** “Any available classical alternative” should include cost, energy, latency, accuracy,
  throughput, and integration—not just faster/cheaper/more accurate in an unconstrained comparison.
- **Current claim:** “No demonstration at production scale” is plausible but time-sensitive and requires a dated
  survey/source.
- **Examples:** Factoring a deployed key is harmful utility, not socially useful; this is conceptually fine but
  could be labeled operational consequence rather than application benefit.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.5 three reasons for the gap

- **Resource range:** “Thousands to millions logical qubits” is likely wrong for many Shor estimates, which often
  need thousands of logical but millions of **physical** qubits. Do not mix levels after emphasizing the
  distinction in the Prelude.
- **Simulation requirements:** Certain useful simulation tasks may need hundreds/thousands logical qubits, not
  necessarily up to millions; separate application classes.
- **Variational absolute:** “Failed to demonstrate empirical advantage” needs defined benchmark and sources.
- **Positive:** Classical algorithm response is an important and often omitted dynamic.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.5 engineer summary

- **Overpersonalization:** “No problem you have at work” cannot be known for every reader; researchers already use
  quantum devices for experiments, calibration, and specialized science workflows.
- **Forecast:** “At least several more years” and “not forever” are both speculative. The latter is faith, not
  established fact.
- **Marketing edge:** Claiming readers will “deploy quantum compute when useful” overpromises the book's role and a
  future deployment opportunity.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** It is a strong ending rhetorically even though precision requires softening.

#### §1.6 moving-target warning

- **Status:** Snapshot says May 2026 and review occurs July 19, 2026. The section explicitly requires
  re-verification; its own publication gate is now triggered by elapsed time and multiple June/July claims
  elsewhere.
- **Source gap:** “Best sources available” is unverifiable without inline/endnote citations or ledger anchors.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.6 “two clean regimes” framing

- **Conceptual defect:** The landscape does not divide cleanly into NISQ and FTQC; analog simulators, annealers,
  error-detected/logical experiments, modular networking, bosonic codes, and early-FT regimes cross the boundary.
  The section itself later calls the transition a gradient, directly contradicting “divides cleanly.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.6 NISQ definition

- **Technical concern:** Runtime budget is not simply set by decoherence; accumulated stochastic errors, leakage,
  calibration drift, measurement/reset latency, and mitigation sampling overhead matter.
- **Definition drift:** Thousands of atoms/qubits do not necessarily mean thousands of fully programmable gate-
  model qubits.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.6 NISQ vendor catalogue

- **Likely factual defect:** IBM Heron is not itself in the “thousand-physical-qubit range”; IBM Condor had 1,121
  qubits, while Heron generations are much smaller/higher quality. “Heron and successors” does not justify the
  parenthetical.
- **Metric misuse:** IonQ's `#AQ 29` is an application/algorithmic benchmark, not a physical qubit count and should
  not sit grammatically beside “H2 at 56.”
- **Platform mismatch:** PsiQuantum is correctly noted as fault-tolerant-targeted, making inclusion under headline
  NISQ devices confusing.
- **Current verification:** Every model name/count/roadmap requires a dated primary citation.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.6 FTQC definition and timeline

- **Overstatement:** Error correction does not make arbitrary-depth circuits literally possible at finite
  resources; it permits arbitrarily long computation with overhead under below-threshold assumptions.
- **Ratio:** “Currently around 1000:1” is not a platform-independent current fact; required ratio varies by physical
  error, logical operation, code distance, target algorithm failure budget, architecture, and factories.
- **Major likely defect:** “Thousands to millions of logical qubits” for cryptographically relevant factoring
  appears to confuse logical and physical counts. Widely cited RSA-2048 estimates use thousands of logical qubits
  and millions of physical qubits, not millions of logical qubits.
- **Timeline:** Years-to-decades estimates must be attributed and should not be presented as a bounded consensus.
- **Positive:** Calling transition a gradient is correct and should replace the opening binary.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.6 physical-to-logical number

- **False precision:** $10^3$–$10^4$ physical qubits per logical qubit “at the error rate needed for Shor” ignores
  magic-state factories, routing, measurement cycles, global algorithm error budget, runtime, and decoder.
- **Unsupported forecast:** “Research targets a tenfold reduction over next several years” needs named roadmaps and
  cannot be treated as expected achievement.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.6 gate-fidelity number

- **Comparison defect:** Top-platform $10^{-4}$ two-qubit error claims vary by randomized benchmarking protocol,
  pair selection, simultaneous operation, leakage, and architecture. Cross-platform ranges need like-for-like
  definitions.
- **Threshold misuse:** A phenomenological ~1% surface-code threshold does not imply a device with average
  two-qubit error below 1% is “well below threshold”; circuit-level correlated errors, measurement/reset, leakage,
  crosstalk, and full repeated code cycles determine below-threshold behavior.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.6 demonstrated logical counts

- **Category defect:** Logical qubit *count* without logical error, operations, lifetime, code, and comparison to
  components repeats the same count fallacy applied earlier to physical qubits.
- **Date mismatch:** “Single to low double digits” conflicts with the Prelude's company-reported 48 Helios logical
  qubits; either reconcile definitions or expose why the counts are not comparable.
- **Competition framing:** “Open competition” turns scientific progress into a race narrative and adds little.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.6 developer implications

- **Security guidance:** PQC migration should occur now because migration and confidentiality timelines are long;
  “not imminent” must not reduce urgency for harvest-now-decrypt-later assets. The paragraph mostly balances this.
- **Forecast:** Five-to-ten-year chemistry usefulness is roadmap optimism, not an evidence-backed baseline. Name
  whose roadmap and a resource-estimate counterview.
- **Category issue:** PQC is adjacent to quantum computing due to threat model but is classical cryptography;
  calling quantum conceptual framework load-bearing there overstates the need for superposition/entanglement
  knowledge.
- **Positive:** “Scenario to plan around, not scheduled deliverable” is excellent calibration.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §1.7 bridge argument summary

- **Major logical overstatement:** Premise (a), “classical computers cannot efficiently simulate quantum systems,”
  is not established generally; scope to generic quantum dynamics under standard complexity assumptions.
- **Speedup wording:** Factoring's exponential advantage is conjectural relative to best classical algorithms;
  query-model separations and simulation advantages require access/task conditions.
- **Trajectory claim:** Hardware trajectory does not by itself establish near-term simulation opportunity or
  medium-term cryptanalytic threat; this is extrapolation.
- **Part labels:** Style asks canonical Roman part names; this file uses “Part 1,” “Part 2,” and “Part 6” together,
  an accepted Arabic shorthand only if not mixed. It is internally consistent Arabic, though less polished than
  the rest of the book's Roman branding.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.7 sanity check 1

- **Pedagogical defect:** The question asks why exponential state specification is an obstruction “rather than a
  feature of any particular algorithm,” reinforcing the false implication that dimension alone proves simulation
  hardness. A correct learner should answer with structured-state exceptions and task/complexity assumptions.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.7 sanity check 2

- **Overconstraint:** Asking for *the word* replacing “parallel” makes “interference” sound like a lexical fix,
  while interference must be engineered and does not describe oracle evaluation by itself.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.7 sanity check 3

- **Classification defect:** Molecular ground-state estimation is not categorically exponential advantage; it
  depends on molecule/family, desired accuracy, state preparation, and best classical method. The question has no
  single defensible answer as written.
- **Factoring:** “Exponential” is believed/relative to best known classical, not unconditional.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §1.7 sanity check 4

- **Pedagogical defect:** Absence of a known speedup is not necessarily caused by a known absent “structural
  feature”; ignorance of algorithms/lower bounds is not a structural proof. Ask for why *current techniques* do not
  apply.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §1.7 sanity check 5

- **Positive:** Useful conceptual distinction with multiple valid reasons.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Footer navigation

- **No defect found:** Links and separator are correct.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### Chapter 1 — second-pass file verdict

- **Overall:** Highly engaging motivation with excellent anti-parallelism and utility/advantage framing, but it
  repeatedly promotes conditional or model-specific claims into universal statements. Many defects mirror the
  Prelude, suggesting centralized claim language is needed.
- **Strong-defect shortlist:** Feynman's non-proof framed as “no”; exponential dimension treated as sufficient
  simulation hardness; original Deutsch result inconsistency; Grover universalized outside query model; four-paper
  opening mismatch; HHL polylog simplification; millions of **logical** qubits confusion; Heron thousand-qubit
  implication; #AQ used beside physical counts; average error below 1% treated as below-threshold; and molecular
  simulation misclassified in the sanity check.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Preserve its speed, contrasts, thought-provoking stakes, and anti-hype voice.
- **Second-pass status:** Complete for this file; every section and the footer were read separately before any next
  manuscript file was opened.

### `book/part-01-orientation/02-classical-to-quantum-contrast.md` — second pass

#### Header, opening contract, and reading guide

- **Inherited defect:** “Certain physical systems are intractable to simulate classically” states conjectural/
  task-specific hardness as fact. Use “appear intractable under standard complexity assumptions.”
- **Reader contract:** “Aim is not precision” is honest, but orientation still must avoid false categorical models.
- **Contradiction:** “Nothing here is load-bearing” conflicts with “conceptual core” and with Chapter 1's bridge
  promising intuition needed for Part II. Say no notation/result is assumed without reintroduction.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.1 classical bit

- **Precision:** Real hardware is analog/noisy underneath abstraction; the paragraph correctly identifies
  bistability/restoration as engineering the digital invariant.
- **Absolute:** “At any moment one ... actual” excludes probabilistic/logical uncertainty but is correct for an
  ideal stored classical state.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.1 qubit parameter count

- **Terminology:** Pure-state space modulo global phase is the Bloch sphere; “configuration space” can include mixed
  states, whose space is the Bloch **ball**.
- **Grammar/math:** “Two real parameters of freedom” should be “two real degrees of freedom.”
- **Conceptual defect:** Relative phase affects subsequent outcomes only under operations/measurements sensitive to
  it, not “every subsequent operation.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.1 measurement and one-sentence contrast

- **Overstatement:** Discreteness is not only at readout; discrete spectra, digital gate choices, syndrome values,
  and quantized physical levels appear internally.
- **Wording defect:** A qubit does not “evaluate to” a value; measurement yields an outcome relative to a chosen
  basis/instrument.
- **Scope:** General measurement need not be computational-basis or binary.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §2.2 deterministic computation

- **Model simplification:** Stateful, interactive, concurrent, nondeterministic-environment programs are not fixed
  $n$-bit functions. Label this circuit/decision-function abstraction.
- **Claim:** “Most production code” is unverifiable and unnecessary.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §2.2 probabilistic computation

- **Matrix convention defect:** A row-stochastic matrix acts on a **row** probability vector; for the column-vector
  convention used elsewhere, the transition matrix is column-stochastic (or transpose the update). Specify
  orientation.
- **State-space simplification:** A machine configuration distribution includes work tape/control state, not merely
  $n$ data bits.
- **Unsupported flourish:** Laptop “many times per second” is plausible but irrelevant.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §2.2 quantum computation

- **Scope:** Pure statevectors/unitaries describe closed gate computation; density matrices/channels are omitted.
- **Norm contrast:** Helpful, though probability vectors can also be represented via square-root amplitudes; phase
  and transformation constraints are the deeper distinction.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.2 interference

- **Major conceptual defect:** The path example adds amplitudes $1/\sqrt2$ and $-1/\sqrt2$ associated with two
  paths, but the classical comparison assigns each probability $1/2`; normalized global processes and distinguishable
  alternatives need specification. As written it is illustrative, not a valid complete stochastic evolution.
- **False theorem:** “Without interference ... BQP collapses to BPP” needs a precise restricted model; deleting
  negative/complex interference does not automatically yield BPP under every definition.
- **Overclaim:** Interference is not singly sufficient and “powers every speedup” is too universal.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §2.2 timeline metaphor

- **Interpretive risk:** “Many timelines” invites many-worlds metaphysics and path literalism. Use computational
  branches/terms in a representation.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.3 irreversible classical computation

- **Landauer defect:** Cost attaches to entropy reduction/logical irreversibility under physical assumptions, not
  automatically “each erased bit” independent of distribution and side information.
- **Unsupported current claim:** Reversible logic has practical research/prototypes; “no mainstream platform” is
  broadly fair but needs no categorical force.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.3 quantum reversibility and measurement

- **Major defect:** Measurement is not the only mechanism for ejecting information; reset, discard/partial trace,
  leakage, noise, and coupling to an environment are nonunitary operations. Modern circuits use mid-circuit
  measurement and reset, not one segregated boundary.
- **Conditional control:** Classical feed-forward after measurement is allowed and important.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.3 Toffoli embedding and uncomputation

- **Technical defect:** You cannot generally run the inverse “on the ancillas” alone; compute–copy/use–uncompute
  applies the inverse to the whole computation/work registers while preserving output through a suitable pattern.
- **Overstatement:** Residual entanglement does not universally “destroy interference at readout”; it can decohere
  the relevant subsystem or prevent intended interference.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §2.3 no-cloning and move-only analogy

- **Proof defect:** A hypothetical cloning map is not simply nonlinear; the contradiction is that linearity/inner-
  product preservation prevents one unitary from cloning arbitrary nonorthogonal states. State the inner-product
  argument.
- **Qualification:** Known orthogonal states can be copied; approximate/probabilistic cloning exists.
- **Analogy concern:** Move-only values can often be reconstructed/copy-enabled by privileged code, while unknown
  states have a physical restriction. Useful but bounded.
- **Cross-reference defect:** Chapter 7 covers entanglement but the no-cloning theorem is listed in Chapter 5
  (§5.13); link the correct location.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §2.4 classical versus quantum gates

- **Precision:** Quantum gates also have exact basis-state truth tables/permutations when classical reversible;
  “matrices, not truth tables” is an overdrawn contrast. Matrices specify coherent action on superpositions.
- **Hadamard defect:** $H$ does not generally “create superposition ... and vice versa”; it maps between $Z$ and
  $X$ bases and can create or remove computational-basis superposition for particular states.
- **CNOT/XOR:** CNOT computes XOR into a target, not merely a “closest relative.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.4 universality and Solovay–Kitaev

- **Technical defect:** Solovay–Kitaev is not “the exact statement” that `{H,T,CNOT}` is universal. Universality of
  Clifford+T and Solovay–Kitaev's efficient approximation for suitable dense inverse-closed sets are related but
  distinct results.
- **Precision:** Approximation is normally up to global phase and a chosen norm/error.
- **Analogy:** NAND functional completeness versus unitary approximation is useful once exact/approximate
  distinction is retained.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.4 countability caveat

- **Positive:** Excellent explanation of why finite quantum libraries approximate continuous unitaries.
- **Nuance:** Some important unitary families/circuits are exactly representable; finite gate sets can exactly
  implement all reversible Boolean functions with ancillas despite not all unitaries.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §2.5 classical/quantum state-space setup

- **Wording defect:** A pure quantum state may have zero amplitudes; it is a linear combination *over* all basis
  states, not necessarily “of all ... simultaneously.”
- **Major conceptual defect:** A classical simulator need not store all amplitudes explicitly; stabilizer, tensor-
  network, decision-diagram, path-sampling, and problem-specific simulations are counterexamples. Say dense
  statevector simulation must.
- **Resource metaphor:** Hardware does not “store amplitudes implicitly” in individually addressable form.
- **Perishable estimate:** 50–60 qubits depends on precision, distributed memory, circuit, and year.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.5 probabilistic comparison

- **Positive:** Correctly says exponential description is insufficient.
- **Theory nuance:** BPP=P is a strong derandomization conjecture supported conditionally; “not believed more than
  polynomially powerful” is awkward because both classes already mean polynomial-time decision. Say randomness is
  not believed to enlarge P.
- **Interference:** Necessary contrast, not by itself a characterization of computational power.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.5 complexity classes

- **Containment precision:** P⊆BPP⊆BQP assumes standard uniform bounded-error definitions; fine at recognition
  level.
- **Factoring defect:** Factoring is not a decision problem without formulation and is in NP∩coNP; cite its
  decision/function version when using it as class witness.
- **Phrase defect:** “Quantum computers do not brute-force NP” is not what BQP/NP containment asks and can confuse
  algorithms with class relations.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §2.6 classical hardware

- **Likely false figures:** Single-bit error “well below $10^{-15}$ per operation,” $10^{10}$ logic operations/s,
  and $10^9$ transistors are workload/device dependent and uncited.
- **False absolute:** Correctness matters greatly in classical hardware (timing, soft errors, ECC, voltage margins,
  aging); power is not “the only number.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.6 platform bullets

- **Superconducting:** They are nonlinear Josephson circuits, not ordinary LC resonators; exact error ranges are
  stale anchors according to the repository audit.
- **Ions:** Not all use only laser pulses; microwave control exists, and all-to-all has scaling costs.
- **Photonics:** “No decoherence in flight” is false/overbroad; loss, mode mismatch, dispersion, dephasing, and
  distinguishability degrade states.
- **Spins:** NV centers are diamond defects, not silicon; “closer to CMOS” applies unevenly.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §2.6 gate cost/budget

- **Math defect:** $10^4$ independent gates at $10^{-3}$ error gives near-total probability of at least one fault,
  but not automatically a randomized state; noise type and circuit sensitivity matter.
- **Metric defect:** Coherence-time/gate-time ratio is not “made precise” as the gate-count budget; gate infidelity,
  crosstalk, measurement, and parallelism dominate.
- **Analogy:** Embedded power-budget framing is useful but quantum errors are not fungible like energy units.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.6 QEC

- **False statement:** Quantum hardware does not need active QEC “always”; NISQ experiments deliberately run
  without it, and whether QEC pays off depends on circuit/error regime.
- **Threshold wording:** “Every operation is above no-error-correction-needed threshold” is not a defined threshold.
- **Ratio:** $10^3$–$10^4$ is task/code/architecture dependent; the million→100–1000 logical arithmetic is only a
  scenario, not general.
- **Cross-reference:** QEC belongs centrally to Chapter 19; Chapter 21 is control/electronics.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.7 measurement

- **Major defects:** Classical reads are not always free/non-destructive; measurement is not the only nonunitary
  operation; quantum measurements can be nondestructive; mid-circuit measurement is not a rare boundary; and the
  unobserved amplitude information is not simply “gone for good” globally.
- **POVM mismatch:** The example is projective computational-basis measurement but prose universalizes it.
- **I/O analogy:** Helpful for preventing casual peeking, yet modern dynamic circuits deliberately measure/reset/
  feed forward repeatedly.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.7 communication

- **Shannon defect:** “$n$ bits transmit at most $n$ bits” requires a noiseless channel/no prior side information;
  compression, shared randomness, and channel uses need defined resource accounting.
- **Dense coding:** One transmitted qubit plus one pre-shared ebit conveys two classical bits; correct once total
  setup resource is explicit.
- **Teleportation:** Saying information moves “without the qubit crossing any wire” is okay, but a quantum system
  (the entanglement half) was distributed earlier.
- **Cross-reference defect:** Chapter 7 §7.12 may discuss resource framing, but teleportation appears to have a
  dedicated figure/section elsewhere; verify exact link.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §2.7 algorithmic toolbox

- **Cross-reference defect:** It assigns Grover/amplitude amplification and phase estimation to Chapter 14, but the
  TOC places landmark algorithms including Grover/Shor in Chapter 15; Chapter 14 is foundational algorithms.
- **Category mixing:** Variational and adiabatic are models/heuristics, not directly parallel reusable primitives.
- **Overstatement:** Toolboxes “rarely solve same problem” ignores hybrid algorithms and quantum speedups of
  classical tasks.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.7 Deutsch problem statement/classical analysis

- **Comparison qualification:** Two queries are required for deterministic exact classical algorithms; bounded-
  error randomized algorithms can use one query with nonzero advantage depending on success criterion.
- **Positive:** Promise and oracle action are clearly stated.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.7 Deutsch quantum walkthrough

- **Historical consistency:** This is the deterministic Deutsch–Jozsa-style refinement, not Deutsch's original
  1985 algorithm discussed accurately in the Prelude. Name the modern refinement.
- **Technical omission:** The circuit needs the initial Hadamards described, oracle, then final H; global phase and
  output-register factorization explain determinism.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §2.7 Deutsch interpretation

- **Major conceptual regression:** “Exponentially many computational paths are run in superposition” is both
  irrelevant for a one-bit/two-input problem and repeats the parallelism misconception the book rejects. Replace
  with phase relation from two oracle branches.
- **False comparison:** It does not beat “every classical one” under all randomized/bounded-error models.
- **Part style:** “Part VI” correctly uses Roman style here.
- **Entertainment: 5/5 despite the defect.**

#### §2.7 bridge to Chapter 3

- **Overclaims:** Interference is not singularly “the resource”; measurement is not the sole irreversible boundary;
  exponential space is not automatically an asset.
- **Positive:** The physical-intuition transition is natural.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §2.7 sanity checks

- **Formatting defect:** STYLE mandates `**Sanity checks before moving on.**` as a bold run-in, not an H2 or
  blockquote. This uses `> **Sanity checks.**` and blockquotes the list, apparently contradicting the documented
  convention; lint unexpectedly accepts it, suggesting an exception or lint gap.
- **Check 2:** Forces “interference” as a one-word answer to an oversimplified comparison.
- **Check 4:** Calls the resource interference and operation phase kickback; useful but ignores promise/model.
- **Check 5 defect:** 99% two-qubit fidelity does not imply a unique typical depth; topology, parallel gates,
  correlated/coherent error, observable, mitigation, and width matter. Comparing this with “depth needed for one
  fault-tolerant logical operation” is undefined.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### Footer navigation and Chapter 2 verdict

- **Navigation:** Correct.
- **Overall:** Concise and engaging, with strong contrasts, but many contrasts are drawn as absolutes that the
  later book itself refutes. The chapter particularly needs an idealized-model label and removal of “only,”
  “every,” and universal Grover/interference claims.
- **Strong-defect shortlist:** stochastic-matrix orientation; measurement as only nonunitary primitive; incorrect
  no-cloning proof summary; wrong no-cloning chapter link; Solovay–Kitaev conflation; explicit-statevector
  simulation universalization; false photonic no-decoherence claim; QEC “always”; Chapter 14 algorithm links;
  exponentially-many-paths line; and nonconforming sanity-check format.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**
- **Second-pass status:** Complete for this file; all sections/footer read separately.

### `book/part-01-orientation/03-physical-intuition.md` — second pass

#### Header and opening purpose

- **Positive:** The practitioner-focused purpose is clear and appropriately distinguishes physical motivation
  from a full quantum-mechanics curriculum.
- **Causal overstatement:** Physics does not uniquely “force those objects” without modeling choices and
  reconstruction assumptions; experiments constrain a formal framework that has proved successful.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Formula-light and empirical-minimality claims

- **False universal:** Not every numerical claim in the chapter is justified locally to one significant figure;
  later platform coherence and SPDC rate ranges are only asserted and several fact-check anchors are stale.
- **False universal:** Named experiments do not all transfer to the gate model “without modification”; translating
  continuous position/momentum, optical polarization, spin, decoherence, and Bell tests into finite-dimensional
  circuits requires abstraction.
- **Overclaim:** Standard postulates are not demonstrably the unique *minimal* description of all observations;
  generalized operational formulations and reconstruction axioms exist.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Three framing points

- **Empirical-force nuance:** “Every postulate ... compressed summary of an experiment” is too tidy. Composite-
  system tensor products, complete positivity, global phase, state-update rules, and ideal unitarity emerge from
  theoretical synthesis as well as direct experiments.
- **Historical-language point:** Excellent warning, though the chapter itself later uses “collapse,” “observer,”
  and classical-limit claims that need the same discipline.
- **Target-intuition defect:** “Projected by measurements” excludes POVMs/instruments and repeats an idealized
  projective-only picture.
- **Interference:** Important mechanism, but making *everything* serve one slogan risks flattening entanglement,
  contextuality, open dynamics, and measurement.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### Reading guide

- **Positive:** Strong cognitive navigation with correct downstream signposts.
- **Independence overclaim:** Sections do use unintroduced eigenvalues, Hilbert spaces, commutators, density
  matrices, partial trace, and channels. Survival definitions help, but “does not depend” should be “does not
  require mastery.”
- **Part naming:** Uses Roman `Part IX`, consistent with canonical style.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.1 opening quantization claim

- **Conceptual defect:** Quantization is not simply “microscopic quantities take only a discrete spectrum.”
  Position can remain continuous; scattering energies are continuous; macroscopic collective systems can have
  discrete spectra. Boundaries/symmetries/operators determine spectra.
- **Angular momentum:** Total/angular-momentum components have discrete eigenvalues, but orbital angular momentum
  and spin require different qualifiers.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.1 Stern–Gerlach setup and classical prediction

- **Historical/physical nuance:** The silver atom's magnetic moment is not a tiny classical arrow with uniformly
  distributed orientations under every classical model; the expected classical pattern also depends on beam
  preparation and field geometry.
- **“Cleanest and most decisive”:** Subjective superlative; atomic spectra are at least as direct a quantization
  demonstration.
- **Positive:** The continuous-smear versus split-beam contrast is visually effective.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.1 observed split and spin interpretation

- **Major technical defect:** Stern–Gerlach measures the silver atom's total magnetic moment/angular momentum,
  dominated by the unpaired electron, but orbital contributions and magnetic-moment signs/g-factors mean detector
  deflection should not casually be equated with measuring the electron's $S_z$ eigenvalues $\pm\hbar/2`.
- **Historical nuance:** Electron spin was identified later; the text says so, but the retroactive account needs
  the atom-versus-electron distinction.
- **False absolute:** “There is no intermediate value, ever” ignores imperfect resolution, transitions, mixed
  beams, and that outcomes concern ideal eigenvalues; say no stable intermediate eigenvalue is observed.
- **Cross-reference oddity:** $\hbar$ is “quantified in §3.10,” far later than first use; define units/value here or
  point to notation/front matter.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.1 qubit analogy

- **Conceptual defect:** Two measurement outcomes do not by themselves imply a two-dimensional state space; a
  two-outcome POVM can act on a higher-dimensional system. The spin-$1/2$ representation supplies dimension two.
- **Hardware nuance:** A Stern–Gerlach apparatus is destructive spatial separation/detection, unlike many
  computational-basis qubit readouts.
- **Positive:** It is a useful operational bridge once labeled an analogy.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.1 closing qualification

- **Positive:** Correctly acknowledges continuous spectra and avoids “everything microscopic is discrete.”
- **Technical defect:** “Photons confined to optical modes” have discrete **photon number** but a mode's phase-space
  quadratures are continuous; “spins confined to a chosen projection axis” is inaccurate—choosing an axis defines
  the measured component, not physical confinement.
- **Overgeneralization:** Qubits can be encoded in anharmonic oscillator levels, topological/parity subspaces, and
  collective codes; not all are simply “contexts in which quantization appears.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.2 wavefunction/state-vector opening

- **Model limitation:** General quantum states may be mixed and require density operators; saying a system “is
  described” by a wavefunction silently assumes a pure state.
- **Terminology:** A wavefunction is a state vector's representation in a continuous basis, not merely an
  alternative name contrasted by finite versus infinite dimension.
- **Born rule:** Outcome amplitude is $\langle m|\psi\rangle` only for rank-one projective measurements; degenerate
  projectors and POVMs need the generalized rule.
- **Observability nuance:** State tomography estimates a state representation from ensembles; “not directly
  observable” is correct but could mention this.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.2 amplitude and phase

- **Conceptual defect:** Phase is not categorically “invisible to a single measurement.” A single measurement in a
  phase-sensitive basis has outcome probabilities depending on relative phase; one *shot* cannot estimate it, but
  the measurement distribution does.
- **Global/relative phase:** The paragraph should distinguish unobservable global phase from observable relative
  phase immediately.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.2 photon polarization/Malus example

- **Positive:** Excellent concrete magnitude-square example.
- **Technical nuance:** A linear polarizer's rejected photon may be absorbed, reflected, or routed depending on
  device; a polarizing beam splitter has two outputs rather than “pass/absorbed.”
- **Amplitude sign/basis:** $\cos\theta` is real only under chosen linear polarization phases; the broader lesson is
  projection amplitude $\langle\theta|H\rangle`.
- **Physical language:** A single photon's “electric-field oscillation direction” is a classical analogy; a Fock
  state has zero field expectation. Say polarization labels its mode/internal degree of freedom.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.2 bookkeeping bullet

- **Major defect:** “Amplitudes only become probabilities at measurement time” sounds like physical conversion;
  probabilities are computed from amplitudes for a specified measurement.
- **Example precision:** $(\alpha,\beta)` and $(e^{i\theta}\alpha,\beta)` have same natural-basis probabilities but
  differ by relative phase only if both components are nonzero. “Any subsequent operation” is false—diagonal
  operations or identity need not reveal it.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.2 complex-number bullet

- **Likely factual defect:** Real-amplitude quantum theory can simulate standard complex quantum computation with
  one extra rebits/dimension under suitable encodings; saying it “does not reproduce observed experiments” without
  assumptions is too strong.
- **Source quality:** A popular book cannot support “complex amplitudes are the unique choice”; quaternionic and
  real quantum theories/reconstruction axioms require specialist references. Complex numbers are not uniquely
  forced absent additional compositional/local-tomography assumptions.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.2 configuration-space bullet

- **Interpretational overstatement:** Whether a wavefunction is “not a field in physical space in any literal
  sense” is interpretation-dependent; for single particles it is a complex field on configuration space, and for
  QFT the ontology differs.
- **Positive:** Warning that multiparticle configuration space is not ordinary 3-space is essential.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.2 polarization closing paragraph

- **Overstatement:** A real polarizing beam splitter is not a **perfect** projective measurement; extinction ratio,
  loss, detector efficiency, mode mismatch, and destructive detection matter.
- **Technical defect:** “Superpositions are intermediate polarizations” is incomplete: real-coefficient
  superpositions yield linear orientations, while relative phases yield elliptical/circular polarization.
  The next phrase partly corrects this but “intermediate” can imply only linear states.
- **False universal:** Not every textbook qubit identity translates cleanly under photon-number/loss/source and
  deterministic two-qubit-gate constraints.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.3 superposition principle

- **Mathematical qualification:** Arbitrary linear combinations belong to the Hilbert space, but superselection
  rules can forbid coherent superpositions across charge/other sectors as physically preparable states.
- **Normalization:** For nonorthogonal states normalization includes cross terms; “once normalized” correctly
  covers this.
- **Interpretation boundary:** “Not secretly up/down” exceeds operational quantum mechanics; hidden-variable
  theories can assign ontic variables while reproducing predictions, though Bell/contextuality constrain them.
  Say the quantum state is not represented as a classical mixture.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.3 Mach–Zehnder setup

- **Beam-splitter precision:** A beam splitter creates a coherent path superposition only with phase conventions;
  “sends it into one of two paths” already sounds like random routing before the paragraph rejects it.
- **Output prediction:** Equal path length does not alone guarantee a particular bright port; beam-splitter
  reflection phases and interferometer alignment determine which detector is dark.
- **Detector idealization:** “Every time/never” assumes lossless optics, indistinguishable paths, perfect
  visibility, and ideal detectors.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.3 “went through both paths” interpretation

- **Interpretational overclaim:** Saying the photon went through both paths is representation-dependent. The
