  standard reductions, NP=coNP would follow; this supports but does not prove the belief.
- **Incomparability belief:** It is common to conjecture incomparable languages, but source it and distinguish evidence
  from fact. Oracle worlds realize multiple relationships.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** It directly dismantles the most common popular misconception.

#### §17.2 — BQP-complete circuit probability problem

- **Promise needed:** “Estimate output probability to inverse-polynomial precision” is not by itself a decision problem.
  A BQP-complete formulation distinguishes promised acceptance probabilities (for example at least 2/3 versus at most
  1/3), or estimates an amplitude/probability under a specified additive-error output model.
- **Potential hardness shift:** Estimating arbitrary circuit probabilities to very fine additive or multiplicative
  precision can enter #P-hard territory. Accuracy and promise determine the class.
- **Reduction notion:** Completeness should specify classical polynomial-time many-one/Turing reductions and whether
  the problem is a promise problem.
- **“Natural”:** Circuit Value/Acceptance is canonical rather than physically natural; “fittingly” works well.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** A class being complete for predicting its own circuits is pleasingly self-referential.

#### §17.2 — physical complete-problem examples

- **Jones polynomial:** Specify link presentation, fifth root, normalization, and additive approximation scale. The
  Aharonov–Jones–Landau result is not arbitrary exact evaluation of “the Jones polynomial.”
- **Local dynamics output defect:** Producing the full state `$e^{-iHt}|\psi_0\rangle$` is not a classical decision
  output. Completeness applies to estimating a measurement probability/local observable, sampling, or implementing the
  evolution under a circuit reduction. State the exact task.
- **Sparse-versus-local:** The bullet says local-Hamiltonian dynamics then calls `$H$` sparse. Local succinct terms imply
  sparsity under conditions, but these are distinct access models.
- **Time/accuracy promises:** Require polynomial `$t$`, succinct oracle/term access, efficiently preparable input, and
  inverse-polynomial or specified constant accuracy.
- **Matrix element:** A complex matrix element requires real/imaginary output or a promise decision and additive
  accuracy. “In `$\mathrm{poly}(t,n)$` time” describes an algorithm rather than a problem definition and omits
  precision/oracle dependence.
- **Connection to Chapter 16:** These corrections should align with the exact BQP-completeness caveat already recorded
  for Hamiltonian simulation there.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Physical complete problems prevent the class discussion from floating away into pure
  formalism.

#### §17.2 — physical interpretation and ECT

- **Class-versus-dynamics overstatement:** BQP contains all efficiently uniform quantum circuits; equating it precisely
  with questions “idealised quantum dynamics can answer” assumes efficient discretization/control and an output model.
- **Thesis naming:** The original Church–Turing thesis concerns computability. The Extended Church–Turing Thesis is
  commonly an efficient-simulation claim, but formulations differ on deterministic versus randomized classical
  machines and polynomial overhead. Define the exact version adopted.
- **“Physically reasonable”:** This phrase is necessarily informal and controversial; analog precision, relativity,
  noise, and exotic models motivate the qualification.
- **Conjecture logic:** If ECT is defined as efficient probabilistic classical simulation of every physical model, a
  scalable quantum computer plus BQP≠BPP would refute it. BQP≠BPP alone is a mathematical separation and needs the
  physical-realizability premise.
- **“Most fundamental”:** Calling this quantum computing’s most fundamental conjecture is editorial. It is a compelling
  candidate, but fault-tolerance/scalability and task-level advantage are separate physical conjectures.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Ending with a challenge to a foundational computing thesis gives the section real grandeur.

#### §17.3 — QMA definition

- **Analogy:** “Quantum analogue of NP” is the standard entry point, but QMA has a quantum verifier as well as a quantum
  witness; QCMA later isolates the verifier-only change.
- **Promise-problem omission:** QMA-completeness is most naturally formulated for promise problems. The completeness and
  soundness bullets should explicitly apply only to promised yes/no instances.
- **Soundness wording:** “No state satisfies probability `>1/3`” correctly means every witness is accepted with
  probability at most `1/3`; use the parallel quantified form for readability.
- **Mixed witnesses:** It suffices to optimize over pure witnesses because acceptance is linear and a maximum occurs on
  a pure state, but a dishonest prover may generally send a mixed state. Mention why the pure-state definition loses
  nothing.
- **Witness description:** A polynomial-qubit state has exponentially many amplitudes and generally lacks an efficient
  classical description; “in general no polynomial-length string describes it” is directionally right, though many
  important QMA witnesses do have succinct circuit/tensor descriptions.
- **Consumption:** The verifier need not preserve the witness and generally cannot clone it. This becomes important for
  QMA amplification, which is subtler than simply requesting copies.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Merlin–Arthur language gives the abstract quantifiers personality.

#### §17.3 — containment chain, MA, and QCMA

- **Chain:** NP⊆MA⊆QCMA⊆QMA⊆PP⊆PSPACE is a useful standard chain.
- **MA precision:** MA has a classical witness followed by a randomized polynomial-time verification protocol with
  completeness/soundness bounds; calling it simply “a BPP verifier” is acceptable but should clarify Merlin cannot
  adapt the witness to Arthur’s private randomness.
- **QCMA input:** A classical polynomial-length witness is given to a uniform quantum polynomial-time verifier. It can
  be copied, unlike a QMA witness, which affects amplification/protocol design.
- **Open equality:** QCMA versus QMA is correctly identified as open.
- **Oracle type:** A “quantum-oracle separation” should be defined; quantum oracles can be unitary transformations, and
  separations relative to them offer different evidence from classical random oracles.
- **Redundant parenthesis:** Since the chain visibly shows QCMA⊆QMA, the “only one way” note adds little; use the space
  to explain why classical witnesses are a restriction.
- **Natural-conjecture wording:** Distinctness is plausible but should be attributed/sourced rather than declared the
  singular natural conjecture.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The classical-witness/quantum-verifier midpoint makes the taxonomy intuitive.

#### §17.3 — local Hamiltonian definition and locality refinements

- **Promise:** The energy gap promise is correctly central; explicitly say inputs satisfying neither case are outside
  the problem.
- **Input encoding:** Require polynomial-bit descriptions/norm bounds for local terms and thresholds. Otherwise
  arbitrary real entries can hide noncomputable information.
- **Normalization:** Standard statements bound each term’s norm polynomially or by one. Complexity depends on the
  promise gap relative to this scaling.
- **Kitaev chronology:** Verify the exact publication/year convention (proof in Kitaev’s work/book and subsequent
  expositions) and the cited 3-local/2-local author attributions.
- **Geometry details:** 2D qubit and 1D qudit completeness results depend on local dimension, interaction type, lattice,
  and sometimes perturbative gadgets. Include those parameters rather than making geometry alone the surprise.
- **Hard versus complete:** “Finding ground-state energy” is an approximation/function task; the promise decision
  version is QMA-complete, while sufficiently accurate energy estimation is QMA-hard under suitable reductions.
- **Physics generalization:** Worst-case local Hamiltonians establish hard instances, not that typical condensed-matter
  calculations are hard or that all interacting systems resist algorithms.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Turning a verifier class into a ground-energy question is a compelling physical payoff.

#### §17.3 — other QMA-complete problems

- **Density consistency:** State the trace-distance promise separating globally consistent from far-from-consistent
  marginals and the locality/subset size. Exact consistency without a gap is not the same computational problem.
- **Strong non-identity defect:** Quantum Circuit Non-Identity Check asks whether a circuit is close to the identity
  (usually up to global phase) or far in an appropriate operator/channel norm under a promise. The parenthetical about
  “restricted to one-output-bit acceptance” is not its definition and does not make it the canonical QMA analogue of
  SAT.
- **Canonical label:** Local Hamiltonian, via the circuit-to-Hamiltonian construction, is usually the flagship/canonical
  QMA-complete problem; declaring a different canonical problem needs justification.
- **Physical-Hamiltonian list:** Hubbard, Heisenberg, and electronic-structure hardness results require specific
  geometries, fields, filling, signs, precision, and coupling promises. A blanket list overstates what each theorem
  proves.
- **DFT consequence:** The Schuch–Verstraete result concerns exact/universal density functionals and specified
  evaluation assumptions. “No efficient universal density functional unless QMA=NP” needs the exact reduction and
  distinction between existence, representation, evaluation, and practical approximations.
- **Complexity equality:** If an NP procedure solved a QMA-hard task under appropriate reductions then QMA⊆NP, and
  NP⊆QMA gives equality; make this reasoning explicit.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The variety conveys reach, although the circuit bullet is likely to misteach the formal
  problem.

#### §17.3 — VQE lesson

- **Core worst-case lesson:** A generic polynomial-time BQP solver for the QMA-complete energy promise problem would
  imply QMA⊆BQP and hence BQP=QMA, since BQP⊆QMA.
- **“VQE cannot” too broad:** A VQE family may have a polynomial runtime guarantee for restricted Hamiltonians,
  approximations larger than the inverse-polynomial promise gap, or outputs other than worst-case ground energy.
  Specify arbitrary local Hamiltonians and the QMA-complete precision.
- **Optimization model:** VQE includes a classical optimizer, sampling noise, ansatz choice, and possibly nonuniform
  advice/training. Explain why the complete end-to-end procedure would still be a BQP/BPP-with-quantum-access
  computation under polynomial resource bounds.
- **“Collapse hierarchy”:** BQP=QMA would be a major class equality, but “the complexity hierarchy” is vague and may
  falsely suggest PH collapses. Name the exact consequence only.
- **Typical-instance claim:** “Most physical systems are not adversarial” is plausible rhetoric, not evidence that
  molecules of interest avoid hard instances. Average-case/distributional performance must be measured.
- **Benchmark tension:** This is a valuable, nuanced conclusion once “works on molecules we care about” is framed as an
  empirical target rather than an established fact.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The direct consequence for VQE makes abstract completeness immediately useful.

#### §17.4 — IP definition and IP=PSPACE

- **Definition:** Include completeness and soundness constants, private/public randomness convention, and polynomial
  communication/rounds. “All-powerful” still means the prover sends only bounded-length messages in the protocol.
- **Analogue wording:** IP generalizes verifier-with-witness interaction rather than being merely “the interactive-proof
  analogue of NP”; NP is the one-message deterministic-verifier special case.
- **Attribution concern:** LFKN established major interactive-proof results (including PH-related protocols), while
  Shamir proved IP=PSPACE. The compound “Shamir–Lund–Fortnow–Karloff–Nisan theorem (1992)” likely conflates papers,
  dates, and the final equality.
- **Why surprising:** A sentence noting that a polynomial-time randomized verifier can validate PSPACE computations
  through arithmetization would make the theorem memorable for a reason beyond superlative.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The weak-verifier/all-powerful-prover reversal is inherently dramatic.

#### §17.4 — QIP and three-message compression

- **Quantum verifier:** QIP permits quantum communication and polynomial-time quantum verification; specify the prover’s
  private space and completeness/soundness against arbitrary strategies.
- **Equality:** QIP=PSPACE is correctly the landmark Jain–Ji–Upadhyay–Watrous result.
- **“No extension at all”:** It does not extend the *set of languages* relative to classical IP, but can alter protocol
  efficiency, message structure, and verifier operations. Qualify the rhetorical absolute.
- **Chronology/attribution:** QIP=QIP(3) is an earlier parallelization result associated with Kitaev–Watrous, not a
  surprising corollary that should appear unattributed after the 2010 equality.
- **Message convention:** “Three messages” can mean prover–verifier–prover transmissions depending on convention;
  define QIP(3) so rounds/messages are not confused.
- **Error preservation:** “Without loss” may require changed completeness/soundness parameters followed by
  amplification. Cite the exact theorem.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Equality with PSPACE and three-message compression deliver two excellent surprises.

#### §17.4 — MIP and MIP*=RE

- **Classical baseline:** MIP=NEXP is correct; give verifier/prover count and note multiple noncommunicating provers.
- **Notation rendering:** Ensure the asterisk in MIP* is consistently escaped or typeset as `$\mathrm{MIP}^*$`, as it
  is here.
- **Protocol-resource precision:** The theorem’s class definition involves finite classical questions/answers generated
  by a polynomial-time verifier and commuting/tensor-product entangled strategies; “polynomially-bounded messages” is
  broad and should match the formal convention.
- **Unbounded entanglement:** Finite-dimensional strategies may require unbounded dimension across accuracy/instances;
  explain that no fixed computable dimension bound suffices rather than implying an actual infinite register is sent.
- **RE definition:** Recursively enumerable languages are those accepted by a Turing machine that may fail to halt on
  no-instances. Calling RE “the arithmetic hierarchy’s first level” should use `$\Sigma_1^0$` and distinguish the
  arithmetical hierarchy from PH.
- **Halting problem:** The standard halting language is RE-complete, so its membership follows, but this does not give a
  computable procedure for deciding non-halting.
- **Connes consequence:** The result refuted Connes’ embedding conjecture via its operator-algebraic equivalences;
  explain enough to prevent readers from thinking a complexity theorem directly solved an unrelated problem by
  coincidence.
- **Tone:** “Conjectured at most NEXP for years” and “few results” are historical assessments needing sources.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** This is among the chapter’s most astonishing results and is presented with appropriate awe.

#### §17.4 — QMA(2)

- **Verifier is not merely told:** The protocol promises/requires two unentangled Merlins to send a product state, but
  the verifier cannot generally certify separability from one copy. Soundness is quantified over allowed product
  witnesses; rephrase “is told.”
- **Mixed/product convention:** Define whether witnesses are pure product states or separable mixtures; extrema and
  shared randomness require care.
- **Containments:** QMA⊆QMA(2) is immediate by ignoring one witness; QMA(2)⊆NEXP is a known upper bound but should be
  cited and its precision/net argument briefly motivated.
- **Time-sensitive open-status:** “Two decades of attack” will age and is not evidence; give a dated status line.
- **Evidence:** Identify the oracle, protocol, or hardness evidence suggesting strictness rather than leaving it vague.
- **Chemistry claim:** Verify the exact “pure-state N-representability” formulation and whether it is QMA(2)-complete,
  QMA-hard, or merely contained/captured; define the consistency/marginal promise.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The possibility that forbidding entanglement increases proof power is deliciously
  counterintuitive.

#### §17.5 — PH definition

- **Definition recursion:** `$\Sigma_k^p=\mathrm{NP}^{\Sigma_{k-1}^p}$` is a serviceable oracle recursion for `$k\geq1$`
  under standard complete-oracle conventions, but define `$\Pi_k^p=\mathrm{co}\Sigma_k^p$` and the equivalent
  alternating-quantifier form promised in prose.
- **Union notation:** Clarify that `$k$` ranges over fixed constants; allowing alternations growing with input would
  reach PSPACE-like power.
- **First level:** The NP/coNP identification is correct.
- **No-separation wording:** No adjacent-level strict separation is known, but avoid the globally broad “no separation
  is proved,” since time/space hierarchies elsewhere do have theorems.
- **Collapse theorem:** If two appropriate adjacent levels coincide, PH collapses; “Karp–Lipton-style consequence ... is
  so strong” is vague. Karp–Lipton specifically says NP⊆P/poly collapses PH to its second level. State the actual
  hypothesis and conclusion or discuss generic PH-collapse consequences without that name.
- **Belief:** Infinite PH is standard but should be framed as conjecture, as it is.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Alternating quantifiers are inherently game-like; an ∃∀ example would make the hierarchy
  vivid.

#### §17.5 — PH versus PSPACE and the motivating question

- **Containment:** PH⊆PSPACE is unconditional because a polynomial number of nested fixed-level quantifiers can be
  searched in polynomial space; for the union, each language has a fixed level.
- **Strictness:** Correctly labeled conjectural.
- **Question framing:** Asking where BQP lies relative to PH is historically and technically motivating.
- **Diagram opportunity:** This chapter urgently needs a containment diagram with solid edges, conjectured dashed
  separations, and oracle-only crossed edges; prose chains are becoming hard to retain.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Brief but sets up the oracle result effectively.

#### §17.5 — Raz–Tal oracle separation

- **Historical overclaim:** “For two decades the standard belief was BQP⊆PH” needs evidence and is likely too sweeping.
  Researchers knew oracle techniques could produce surprising relations; absence of an oracle separation is not a
  consensus containment conjecture.
- **What was proved:** Raz–Tal gave an oracle relative to which BQP is not contained in PH, using a forrelation-related
  distribution/separation. State classical versus random oracle and the precise almost-sure/existence formulation.
- **“Demolished” calibration:** An oracle separation rules out relativizing containment proofs and changes evidence; it
  does not refute the unrelativized conjecture BQP⊆PH. The next sentence acknowledges this, so use “overturned that
  relativized intuition.”
- **Entire PH:** Relative to the constructed oracle, noncontainment in the union PH is indeed stronger than separation
  from any one fixed level.
- **Unrelativized candidate:** No explicit natural language is thereby known to lie in BQP outside PH. Make this clear.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The result is a superb illustration of why oracle evidence matters and why it must not be
  mistaken for an unconditional separation.

#### §17.5 — BQP versus PSPACE

- **Containment proof wording:** “Path-integral simulation” may be confused with physical/Feynman path integrals; “sum-
  over-computational-paths polynomial-space simulation” is clearer.
- **Strictness:** Correctly marked conjectural and unproved.
- **Comparison context:** Since BQP⊆PP⊆PSPACE was already established, reference the tighter chain rather than making
  the path argument appear new.
- **Relativized evidence:** If relevant, mention known oracle separations BQP versus PSPACE carefully; otherwise the
  two-sentence paragraph is sufficient.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Clear, but mostly repetition.

#### §17.5 — BQP with a BQP oracle

- **Oracle semantics:** `$\mathrm{BQP}^{\mathrm{BQP}}$` is subtle because a language oracle is an exact coherent unitary
  while a BQP subroutine is bounded-error. The closure proof amplifies each polynomially many subroutine calls enough,
  coherently implements them, and controls total error.
- **“Simplest fact”:** Closure under BQP subroutines is important, but not trivial enough to wave away the coherent-
  error issue.
- **Composition:** Subroutines do not literally “collapse”; they inline with polynomial size after uniform generation
  and error reduction.
- **NP comparison:** `$\mathrm{NP}^{\mathrm{NP}}=\Sigma_2^p$` is a conventional oracle characterization; the inequality
  from NP is conditional, as the text says.
- **Hierarchy conclusion overreach:** BQP closure under its own oracle does not by itself explain why no quantum
  hierarchy can be defined; alternating quantum/classical quantifiers and proof systems create QPH-like hierarchies.
- **“Right hierarchy”:** There is no single universally accepted right quantum hierarchy, and QMA/QCMA plus
  counterparts need formal alternation definitions. Present them as studied candidates, not the answer.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Self-oracle closure is an appealing contrast with NP once the error bookkeeping is exposed.

#### §17.6 — query-complexity framing

- **Definition:** The number of coherent input-oracle uses is correctly isolated as query complexity, but the oracle’s
  unitary action, inverse availability, output alphabet, and promise must be specified for each problem.
- **Why useful:** Sharp black-box lower bounds are indeed a major advantage of the model.
- **Polynomial method attribution:** Verify the full Beals–Buhrman–Cleve–Mosca–de Wolf author list, year, and whether the
  intended theorem concerns exact degree or bounded-error approximate degree.
- **Adversary lineage:** Separating Ambainis’s original positive adversary, the negative-weight general adversary bound,
  and Reichardt’s tightness result is historically useful.
- **“Tight for many”:** Polynomial and adversary methods have different reach; the general adversary bound characterizes
  bounded-error quantum query complexity for finite functions up to constants, whereas ordinary polynomial degree does
  not always. State this stronger precise fact later rather than blending the tools.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Lower bounds give the section a satisfying sense that claims can finally be pinned down.

#### §17.6 — Deutsch–Jozsa entry

- **Quantum count:** One exact quantum query is correct in the standard phase/kickback-compatible oracle model.
- **Classical deterministic bound:** The exact worst-case deterministic query complexity is `$2^{n-1}+1$`, so
  `$\Omega(2^n)$` is asymptotically correct but unnecessarily imprecise in a catalogue of sharp results.
- **Randomized claim:** Bounded-error randomized classical algorithms use a constant number of samples for constant
  error because two differing outputs disprove constancy and repeated equal outputs make balance unlikely. Give the
  actual error after `$k$` samples.
- **Promise importance:** The exponential-looking deterministic separation relies completely on the constant-versus-
  exactly-balanced promise. This is worth emphasizing as the first lesson in reading oracle claims.
- **Input size ambiguity:** `$n$` is the query-string length and the truth table has size `$N=2^n$`; explicitly naming
  both prevents exponent confusion across catalogue entries.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The bounded-error deflation is an excellent skeptical twist.

#### §17.6 — Bernstein–Vazirani entry

- **Quantum count:** One query recovers the entire hidden string in the standard oracle model, with Hadamards around the
  phase query.
- **Classical count:** Deterministically, querying basis vectors recovers `$s$` in `$n$` queries, and the information
  lower bound gives `$\Omega(n)$`; specify whether the theta claim is deterministic/exact or bounded-error randomized.
- **Output-information nuance:** One query yielding `$n$` classical bits is possible because the oracle answer depends
  on an `$n$`-bit query in superposition; this is an excellent place to dispel the misleading “read all values at once”
  story.
- **Dot product:** Define `$s\cdot x$` over `$\mathbb F_2$`.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Compact and surprising, though a single explanatory clause would distinguish it from magic.

#### §17.6 — Simon entry

- **Quantum queries:** `$O(n)$` oracle queries plus polynomial classical linear algebra is standard; success probability
  and rank-collection repetitions should be stated.
- **Classical lower bound:** The birthday-paradox `$\Omega(2^{n/2})$` randomized-query lower bound depends on the
  two-to-one XOR-period promise and black-box access.
- **Speedup label:** Exponential in `$n$` and superpolynomial in the query-input length is correct, but the oracle’s full
  table has exponential size; distinguish succinct problem parameter from explicit input length.
- **Historical link:** Simon strongly inspired Shor’s period-finding approach, but “direct inspiration” is a historical
  claim best supported by Shor/Simon sources.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** It is the catalogue’s cleanest leap from oracle separation to algorithmic history.

#### §17.6 — Grover entry

- **Bounds:** `$\Theta(\sqrt N)$` bounded-error quantum and `$\Theta(N)$` randomized classical query complexities for
  unique/marked-item search are standard, with dependence on number of marked items if generalized.
- **Attribution:** BBBV supplies the hybrid lower-bound lineage; Zalka later proved sharp optimality constants. Mention
  the result matching the intended strength.
- **Decision versus search:** OR decision and finding a marked location have related bounds but different outputs;
  identify which problem the catalogue means.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Familiar but essential as the polynomial-speedup anchor.

#### §17.6 — Forrelation entry

- **Convention risk:** “One quantum query” depends on whether a query to each of two functions is counted separately or
  a combined phase-oracle call is allowed. Standard presentations often count one query to each oracle. Define the
  model.
- **Lower-bound verification:** The transition from the original `$\Omega(N^{1/4})$` bound to a claimed tight
  `$\widetilde\Omega(\sqrt N)$` needs exact citations, log factors, promise/distribution, and upper bound.
- **Attribution/date:** Aaronson introduced Forrelation in work around 2009–2010; verify publication year and which
  Aaronson–Ambainis result supplies the refinement.
- **Raz–Tal relation:** Raz–Tal used a variant/generalized forrelation distribution, not necessarily the exact
  one-query promise problem in this bullet. Describe the connection without saying one result literally underlies the
  other.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** It usefully introduces the less famous separation that later powers a major oracle theorem.

#### §17.6 — lifting query separations to time

- **Central warning:** This is one of the chapter’s most important practitioner lessons.
- **Shor oracle terminology:** Modular exponentiation is an efficiently implementable function subroutine within
  period finding, not an externally given black-box oracle in the same sense as a relativized complexity oracle.
- **Reversible map:** XORing `$a^x\bmod N$` into a sufficiently sized target is unitary; resource cost depends on
  reversible modular multiplication, exponent size, arithmetic design, and desired gate set.
- **Advantage status:** Efficient implementation lets Shor retain polynomial quantum runtime, but the *separation* from
  classical time remains conditional on the absence of a polynomial classical factoring/discrete-log algorithm.
- **Random-oracle wording:** A random oracle has no known succinct generic implementation; an exponential truth table
  can still be implemented with exponential resources. Say “no polynomial-size implementation for a typical random
  oracle,” not “no efficient implementation exists” without defining probability/model.
- **Beyond implementation:** State preparation, output extraction, and non-query computation can also erase a query
  advantage even when each oracle call is cheap.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The modular-exponentiation contrast turns an abstract caveat into a concrete evaluation tool.

#### §17.6 — polynomial method

- **Degree theorem:** For a standard Boolean-input oracle, every measurement outcome probability after `$T$` queries is
  a real multilinear polynomial of degree at most `$2T$` in input bits. Amplitudes have degree at most `$T$`.
- **Representing versus approximating:** Exact algorithms yield exact representing polynomials; bounded-error algorithms
  yield polynomials approximating the target on its (possibly promised) domain. The text should not say merely “any
  representing polynomial” for both.
- **Partial functions:** Promise problems can have much lower approximate degree on the promise domain than their total
  extensions; this matters for Deutsch–Jozsa, Simon, and Forrelation.
- **Lower-bound conversion:** `$T\geq\widetilde{\deg}(f)/2$` is the clean bounded-error statement for Boolean functions.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Translating algorithms into low-degree polynomials is an elegant and memorable lower-bound
  idea.

#### §17.6 — adversary method and section conclusion

- **Oversimplified definition:** Adversary matrices relate pairs of inputs with differing function values and track
  progress under queries; they are not restricted to pairs differing in exactly one position. The one-position
  description resembles a basic hybrid/sensitivity intuition and understates negative weights.
- **Negative weights:** Allowing signed adversary matrices overcomes limitations of the original nonnegative method;
  briefly state the ratio/spectral-norm form or link to a formal source.
- **Tightness scope:** The general adversary bound characterizes bounded-error quantum query complexity up to constant
  factors for finite-domain functions (with suitable output generalizations). Qualify “all functions.”
- **“Together”:** Tightness comes specifically from the general adversary bound plus span-program/algorithmic
  constructions, not from the polynomial and adversary methods jointly.
- **“Tight bounds are the norm”:** Many important partial functions and concrete problems still have gaps; this is
  energetic but overly universal rhetoric.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The promise of matching upper and lower bounds is satisfying, though the adversary mechanism
  needs one more line of substance.

#### §17.7 — oracle-separation definition

- **Definition:** Correct once the oracle type and access convention are fixed.
- **Class equality nuance:** To separate containments one often shows `$\mathcal C^O\nsubseteq\mathcal D^O$`, stronger
  and more informative than mere inequality when the reverse containment is not automatic.
- **Evidence direction:** An oracle separation shows that any proof of the corresponding unrelativized containment/
  separation cannot be wholly relativizing. It is a barrier to proof technique, not direct evidence that the
  unrelativized classes differ.
- **Nonrelativizing definition:** Exploiting “specific properties of the unrelativized model” is intuitive; examples
  such as arithmetization/algebraization would make it concrete.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The concept immediately explains why oracle results matter without pretending they settle the
  real world.

#### §17.7 — Baker–Gill–Solovay reference point

- **Statement:** The existence of oracles giving both P=NP and P≠NP relativized worlds is the standard barrier result.
- **Inference precision:** It shows no proof resolving P versus NP can consist solely of techniques that hold relative
  to every oracle. It does not show current techniques cannot be combined with nonrelativizing ingredients.
- **Notation:** Reuse one oracle letter convention (`$A,B,O$`) consistently.
- **Historical citation:** Verify year/title and provide a bibliography key.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Two contradictory oracle worlds make the methodological barrier vivid.

#### §17.7 — BPP versus BQP oracle bullet

- **Direction:** Since BPP⊆BQP relativizes for standard classical oracles, “not equal” means a language in BQP outside
  BPP; write the directional noncontainment.
- **Attribution suspicion:** Verify that Bernstein–Vazirani 1993 is the exact oracle separation intended and whether it
  uses recursive/classical or quantum oracle access.
- **Random-oracle claim:** Aaronson’s 2010 result may concern Fourier Fishing/Forrelation-style oracle distributions;
  confirm whether it truly establishes separation relative to a uniformly random oracle, rather than an oracle drawn
  from a structured distribution.
- **Model:** Distinguish classical Boolean oracles accessible in quantum superposition from genuinely quantum/unitary
  oracles.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** This is the most direct relativized evidence for quantum computational advantage.

#### §17.7 — NP versus BQP oracle bullet

- **Precise statement:** Give the probability-one random-oracle formulation if that is BBBV’s theorem, including
  relativized superscripts on both classes.
- **Faulty intuition phrase:** Oracle NP is not generically “structured search”; the random-oracle language is designed
  so a witness certifies existence while quantum queries face unstructured search. Calling all oracle NP structured
  search reverses the relevant point.
- **Grover limitation:** The lower bound gives quadratic improvement for black-box existence/search, not a theorem
  about arbitrary NP structure.
- **Citation:** BBBV’s paper combines oracle lower bounds and quantum-strength/weakness results; identify the theorem.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** It correctly resists the myth that superposition makes existential search polynomial.

#### §17.7 — BQP versus PH and NP^NP bullets

- **Raz–Tal:** The PH noncontainment relative to an oracle is the landmark statement already reviewed in §17.5.
- **Redundancy:** Repeating it is reasonable in a catalogue, but cross-reference rather than re-explaining.
- **Consequence:** Because `$\mathrm{NP}^{\mathrm{NP}}=\Sigma_2^p\subseteq\mathrm{PH}$` relative to the same oracle
  under consistent oracle conventions, BQP not contained in PH implies noncontainment in NP^NP. Show this one-line
  logic.
- **Oracle nesting nuance:** Superscript notation such as
  `$(\mathrm{NP}^O)^{\mathrm{NP}^O}$` versus `$\mathrm{NP}^{\mathrm{NP},O}$` can differ in presentation; state the
  intended relativized level.
- **Catalogue value:** The separate NP^NP bullet adds little once the entire-PH result is present unless it connects
  explicitly to familiar two-level algorithms.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Correctly dramatic but repetitive.

#### §17.7 — QMA versus QCMA oracle bullet

- **Oracle type is essential:** Aaronson–Kuperberg uses a quantum oracle separation; emphasize that classical-oracle
  separation remains a different/open evidentiary standard if applicable.
- **Witness conclusion:** It shows quantum witnesses can outperform classical witnesses in that relativized model, not
  that they are strictly more powerful unconditionally.
- **“Suggesting”:** Appropriately cautious, though “only relative” repeats the section’s general warning.
- **Later refinements:** §17.3 mentioned refinements while this bullet does not; harmonize citations.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The witness question gives oracle separations a distinct operational meaning.

#### §17.7 — unconditional coarse separations

- **Category wording:** P≠EXP is classical rather than specifically an “unconditional separation in quantum
  complexity”; it is included as a reference baseline.
- **BQP versus EXPSPACE:** From BQP⊆PSPACE and PSPACE⊊EXPSPACE, BQP⊊EXPSPACE follows correctly: every BQP language lies
  in PSPACE and some EXPSPACE language lies outside PSPACE.
- **Hierarchy theorem:** Name the deterministic space hierarchy and any constructibility assumptions.
- **BQP versus EXP:** It is indeed not known whether all EXP languages can be solved in BQP. If BQP=EXP, the containments
  BQP⊆PSPACE⊆EXP would force PSPACE=EXP.
- **“Merely”:** PSPACE=EXP would be a major open collapse, not a minor consequence; remove “merely.”
- **Missing known separations:** Clarify whether other unconditional separations involving restricted quantum classes,
  query models, communication complexity, or interactive proofs are intentionally excluded.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The coarse-separation joke lands, but “merely” minimizes a striking collapse.

#### §17.7 — evidence assessment

- **Honest status:** BPP versus BQP remains unproved and not close under current general techniques.
- **Factoring/discrete log:** These are in BQP and lack known polynomial classical algorithms, but their candidacy is
  evidence from algorithmic history, not hardness theory.
- **Hamiltonian simulation:** Specify a decision/sampling/observable problem and access model; generic implementation of
  a unitary is not itself a language in BQP\BPP.
- **Sampling mismatch:** Sampling advantages do not directly witness a decision-language separation BQP≠BPP without
  reductions/complexity assumptions.
- **Absence of algorithms:** Failure to find classical algorithms is weak, historically defeasible evidence; the text
  appropriately labels the conclusion belief but could rank evidence strengths.
- **Conditional separations:** Some oracle/query/communication separations are unconditional *within their models*;
  for real-time class separation, cryptographic and PH noncollapse assumptions support particular tasks, not generally
  BPP≠BQP.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The candid epistemic inventory is one of the chapter’s strongest passages.

#### §17.8 — taxonomy framing

- **Purpose:** Separating polynomial, superpolynomial, restricted-model, and sampling claims is extremely useful.
- **Overlap:** These categories are not mutually exclusive: Simon is superpolynomial and query-model-restricted;
  sampling is an output type, not a growth rate. Present a two-axis table (scaling versus computational model/output).
- **Baseline:** Every speedup also needs classical comparator, error, confidence, access, preprocessing, and output
  definitions; preview these dimensions.
- **Spelling:** “Taxonomise” follows British style; harmonize with the project’s technical spelling policy.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** A claim taxonomy promises immediate practical payoff.

#### §17.8 — polynomial-speedup examples

- **“Provable, robust, ubiquitous”:** Query-model speedups are provable, but end-to-end robustness and ubiquity are
  separate empirical statements. This opening contradicts the later lifting caveat.
- **Grover:** Correct in the oracle model; total runtime depends on reversible oracle and diffusion costs.
- **Element distinctness:** The `$O(n^{2/3})$` quantum query result should include the classical comparator and matching
  lower bound if the section calls the speedup.
- **Triangle finding:** Verify `$\widetilde O(n^{5/4})$` against the current best query/time algorithms, input model
  (adjacency matrix), and lower bounds. A lone exponent ages badly.
- **Monte Carlo notation defect:** “`$O(\sqrt T)$` speedups” does not define `$T$`. If a classical method uses `$T$`
  samples/steps for an error target, amplitude estimation may use roughly `$\sqrt T$` coherent queries under bounded
  integrand/state-preparation assumptions.
- **Graph/number-theory claim:** “Many” needs representative examples or removal.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The list feels energetic, but inconsistent variables make it less reference-ready.

#### §17.8 — lifting and practical heuristic

- **Universal lifting defect:** These speedups do not all “survive lifting” automatically. Oracle construction, QRAM,
  walk setup/update/check cost, memory, reversible arithmetic, and output can dominate.
- **Fault-tolerance condition:** Constant overhead is unrealistic phrasing; error-correction overhead depends on problem
  size, target failure probability, code distance, architecture, and circuit structure, though it may be
  polylogarithmic/asymptotically manageable.
- **Arbitrary threshold:** `$n\geq10^9$` has no universal meaning because `$n$` may be vertices, database entries,
  samples, or runtime units and per-query costs vary by orders of magnitude.
- **Square-root crossover:** Compare concrete classical cost `$c_C n$` against quantum
  `$c_Q\sqrt n$` plus setup/readout; crossover is around `$(c_Q/c_C)^2` only in a simplified model.
- **Oracle cost:** Calling it “cheap relative to the rest” is backwards if oracle calls dominate both algorithms; what
  matters is coherent quantum query cost relative to classical evaluation and the non-query overhead.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Readers want a rule of thumb, but this one masquerades as universal despite missing units.

#### §17.8 — superpolynomial-speedup framing and Simon/Shor

- **Conditional status:** No unconditional unrelativized time separation between BPP and BQP is known; correct.
- **Simon:** Its exponential query separation is proved, while a time advantage requires an efficiently implementable
  structured oracle/problem.
- **Shor scaling:** Against the best known subexponential factoring algorithms, polynomial quantum factoring is
  superpolynomial in input bit length, not conventionally a proven exponential separation.
- **Assumption wording:** “No efficient classical factoring algorithm exists” is exactly the unproved condition; RSA
  provides historical practical evidence but not a formal hardness assumption at all parameter regimes.
- **Discrete log:** Classical complexity depends strongly on the group; some groups admit easier algorithms. Scope the
  comparison to standard hard families.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The distinction between oracle proof and real-time belief is compelling.

#### §17.8 — abelian HSP and Hallgren examples

- **HSP complexity:** Abelian HSP has efficient quantum algorithms when group operations/representations and the QFT are
  efficiently realizable; it is not uniformly “superpolynomial” versus classical algorithms for every abelian group
  and hiding function.
- **Group-action wording:** Standard HSP assumes oracle access to a function constant and distinct on subgroup cosets;
  “group action” describes related hidden-shift/action problems and should not replace the oracle definition.
- **Time lifting:** Efficient quantum implementation is necessary, while a superpolynomial classical lower bound remains
  conjectural for natural instances.
- **Pell/principal ideals:** Hallgren gives polynomial-time quantum algorithms for specified number-field problems.
  State the best known classical baseline, output/precision, fixed-degree assumptions if any, and whether the speedup is
  proved or based on absence of classical polynomial algorithms.
- **QFT commonality:** The periodic/abelian Fourier structure is a useful unifying observation.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The less famous number-theoretic examples broaden the reader’s view beyond factoring.

#### §17.8 — nonabelian-HSP outlook

- **“Sporadic”:** Superpolynomial quantum algorithms outside abelian Fourier methods exist or are candidates in
  simulation, sampling, topological invariants, and algebra; the statement needs a model/output qualifier.
- **Problem diversity:** Dihedral and symmetric HSP have very different status—subexponential algorithms and relevance
  to lattice problems versus graph isomorphism barriers. Avoid grouping them as uniformly untouched.
- **Chronology:** “Two decades” is dated and undersells longer/shorter lines depending on the problem.
- **Centrality:** Calling generic nonabelian HSP a central open problem is defensible historically but should be
  presented as a community perspective with sources.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The unsolved frontier supplies tension after the success stories.

#### §17.8 — exponential speedups in restricted models

- **Hamiltonian baseline:** Comparing quantum evolution to full classical state-vector storage gives exponential
  scaling in qubits, but useful observables may be classically accessible without storing the state.
- **“Right” baseline:** No single right classical method exists; tensor networks, sign-problem-free Monte Carlo,
  semiclassics, Krylov methods, stabilizer decompositions, and problem-specific solvers define a best-known portfolio.
- **Hardness:** Specify Hamiltonian family, time, initial state, observable/sample output, error, and classical
  conjecture before calling the speedup exponential.
- **HHL:** Correctly flags block-encoding/input-access assumptions, but conditioning, rank, precision, state
  preparation, and classical-output restrictions also do essential work.
- **Section reference:** §17.12 has not yet been read in this pass; verify it actually makes these conditions explicit
  rather than repeating the slogan.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The baseline correction is exactly the skepticism the taxonomy needs.

#### §17.8 — sampling-complexity framing

- **Distinct output model:** Correctly distinguishes sampling from decision/function output.
- **Approximation metric:** Define whether “close” means total-variation distance, cross-entropy fidelity, heavy-output
  generation, linear cross-entropy benchmarking, or another metric; hardness changes drastically.
- **Single versus repeated sample:** Complexity includes circuit instance generation and producing enough independent
  samples for a verifier/statistic, not just one draw.
- **Verification:** Demonstrating closeness to a classically intractable distribution is itself difficult; include this
  as a central experimental constraint.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The new output type expands the reader’s mental model of computational tasks.

#### §17.8 — BosonSampling bullet

- **Model details:** Standard BosonSampling injects single photons into a passive linear-optical network and measures
  output occupation patterns; loss, collisions, distinguishability, and detector model matter.
- **Hardness oversimplification:** Approximate-sampling hardness relies on multiple conjectures, including average-case
  hardness of Gaussian permanents and anti-concentration-type assumptions, plus a PH-collapse argument. It does not
  follow merely from “conjectured #P-hardness of approximating permanents.”
- **Typography:** Use `$\#\mathrm P$` or the project’s math convention rather than inline code ``#P``.
- **Year/citation:** Verify 2011 preprint/publication convention and give the exact result.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Photons and permanents provide a vivid alternative to circuit algorithms.

#### §17.8 — IQP bullet

- **Definition:** IQP circuits use commuting gates diagonal in one basis (often X-diagonal when surrounded by
  Hadamards); “diagonal-in-X-basis polynomial-size constructions” is directionally correct but should specify input and
  measurement.
- **Acronym:** “Instantaneous Quantum Polynomial-time” is the usual expansion; verify capitalization and hyphenation.
- **Hardness logic:** Efficient approximate classical sampling under the specified error notion plus average-case
  conjectures can collapse PH to a finite level. PH noncollapse alone is not always the sole assumption.
- **Citation:** Include year and exact Bremner–Jozsa–Shepherd theorem.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The paradox of commuting circuits being classically hard is inherently engaging.

#### §17.8 — random-circuit-sampling bullet

- **Family definition:** RCS requires an ensemble, geometry, depth, gate distribution, noise model, and approximation/
  scoring task; “the family” is too singular.
- **Hardness ingredients:** Anti-concentration and PH noncollapse are not alone sufficient; average-case #P-hardness of
  output-probability estimation and the sampling-to-counting reduction are load-bearing conjectures.
- **Experiment reference:** Name/cite the 2019 Google paper and distinguish its experimental benchmark from an
  asymptotic RCS hardness theorem.
- **Noise:** Constant physical noise can make distributions close to classically easy ones in some regimes; hardness of
  noisy sampling is a central unresolved condition.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** It connects abstract sampling to recognizable hardware history.

#### §17.8 — why sampling is attractive

- **Fault-tolerance overclaim:** A demonstration need not use full fault tolerance, but arbitrary noisy modest-depth
  circuits do not suffice. Output fidelity must remain high enough for the defined hard task and verifiable statistic.
- **Noise-versus-hardness tension:** The same noise that makes circuits implementable can wash out the interference
  supporting hardness. State this explicitly.
- **Strong logical defect:** The sentence says the hardness conjectures are “weaker than BQP≠BPP” and “would not collapse
  PH if violated.” Usually the claimed argument is: if an efficient classical sampler exists *and* the average-case/
  anti-concentration conjectures hold, PH collapses. If a conjecture itself is false, no collapse follows. This does not
  order those conjectures cleanly as weaker than BQP≠BPP.
- **Belief evidence:** “Widely believed” should attach to each precise conjecture; anti-concentration may be provable for
  ensembles while average-case hardness remains conjectural.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The experimental appeal is clear, but the load-bearing logic is currently misstated.

#### §17.8 — terminology of supremacy and advantage

- **Historical definition:** Preskill’s coinage and broad milestone idea should be checked against the original 2012
  text; “any task no classical computer could replicate in reasonable time” may omit usefulness/error qualifiers.
- **Reason for terminology shift:** “Less politically loaded” is vague and understates objections to the word’s social/
  historical connotations. Cite style/community discussions or simply note current preferred usage.
- **Concept not identical:** “Quantum advantage” can mean experimental computational advantage, asymptotic advantage,
  or practical useful advantage; it is not always exactly synonymous with the original supremacy milestone.
- **Best-known baseline:** Faster than the best known classical code/hardware is an empirical record, not proof that no
  classical algorithm can do it.
- **Task precision:** Excellent emphasis on a specific well-defined task; add matched error, energy, cost, and
  verification conditions.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The terminology history is useful and accessible.

#### §17.8 — original Sycamore claim and IBM response

- **Google numbers:** Verify the precise reported estimate—roughly 200 seconds versus 10,000 years for a particular
  classical simulation method—and distinguish sample generation from verification/cross-entropy estimation.
- **IBM rebuttal:** IBM’s proposal estimated days using disk/storage on Summit rather than actually completing the full
  simulation; label projection versus demonstrated run.
- **Resource equivalence:** Quantum wall time, calibration/postselection, classical simulation fidelity, storage, and
  energy must be matched.
- **“Rapidly contested”:** Historically fair but cite both primary papers and dates.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The collapse from millennia to days is a perfect demonstration of moving baselines.

#### §17.8 — 2021–2024 classical RCS claims

- **Primary-source audit essential:** Every named team, year, machine, GPU count, runtime, sample fidelity, and
  “surpassing” comparison is time-sensitive and should be checked against papers rather than press reports.
- **Apples-to-apples risk:** Tensor contraction studies may compute amplitudes, batches, correlated samples, or samples
  at different fidelity/XEB, not exactly reproduce Google’s sampling task. Normalize fidelity and number/independence
  of samples.
- **Sunway wording:** Verify whether the Gordon Bell run’s “304 seconds” refers to one amplitude, a sample batch,
  contraction stage, or an estimated equivalent task, and whether it actually used the Wuxi new-generation machine.
- **GPU claim:** “Higher-fidelity samples several times faster than Sycamore itself” is an exceptionally strong claim;
  verify end-to-end runtime, target circuit set, number of samples, XEB, and whether the quantum device’s 200 seconds
  excluded setup.
- **Technology dating:** “Methods that did not exist” may be false; tensor networks existed, while particular slicing/
  contraction/path optimizations improved. Say the methods were substantially improved.
- **Narrative balance:** Later quantum processors/circuit depths also advanced; recounting only classical rebuttals can
  bias the historical conclusion.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The computational arms race is gripping, provided the comparisons survive forensic checking.

#### §17.8 — Jiuzhang assessment

- **Experiment detail:** Gaussian Boson Sampling uses squeezed-light inputs and threshold/photon-number detection
  variants; distinguish Jiuzhang generations and their actual task.
- **Strong superlative:** “Strongest unrebutted advantage demonstrations” is subjective, temporally unstable, and
  requires comparison with photonic, ion, neutral-atom, and later superconducting experiments.
- **“Unrebutted”:** Classical spoofing/simulation papers may challenge validation or shrink estimates without fully
  matching the task; define what counts as rebuttal and conduct a current primary-source search.
- **Hardness assumptions:** Different from RCS does not mean stronger or safer. Gaussian loss, distinguishability,
  threshold detection, and validation affect hardness.
- **Versions/dates:** Verify Jiuzhang 2020 and 3.0 2023 citations and reported advantage metrics.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The photonic counterpoint keeps the history from becoming a one-platform story.

#### §17.8 — 2024–2025 “honest summary”

- **Dating:** A 2024–2025 summary is stale relative to the review date and should be a clearly labeled source-cutoff
  snapshot, not evergreen prose.
- **Universal pursuit claim:** “Every claimed advantage demonstration” is almost certainly false; not every experiment
  has a dedicated classical-simulation team or comparable public rebuttal.
- **Gap direction:** The RCS gap may narrow for a fixed old benchmark while newer quantum circuits increase scale/depth.
  “Narrowing rather than widening” needs a defined moving or fixed benchmark.
- **Classical feasibility:** “Expensive but feasible” depends on time, energy, memory, fidelity, and access to leadership-
  scale supercomputers; define practical feasibility.
- **Epistemic tone:** Calling it honest implies alternative summaries are dishonest. Use “dated assessment based on
  [sources].”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Candid uncertainty is valuable, but sweeping status claims weaken trust.

#### §17.8 — practitioner’s three questions

- **Question 1:** Exact task identification is essential; add input ensemble, size, noise/error metric, sample count,
  and verification statistic.
- **Question 2:** Strong versus weak simulation is valuable terminology but should be defined. Also ask whether the
  baseline is best known asymptotically, best implemented, or hardware-limited.
- **Question 3:** “Rebutted” is not binary; later classical work can narrow, alter, spoof, or match only a metric.
  Encourage a table of comparable fidelity/runtime/resources.
- **False frequency:** “Almost always within 18–24 months” is an unsupported universal statistic and likely false.
  Remove it or back it with a complete dataset and definition of claimed demonstrations/rebuttals.
- **Cryptographic analogy:** Both fields update security/advantage estimates against adversarial algorithmic progress,
  but cryptography often has formal games/reductions that experimental sampling claims lack. The analogy is useful if
  bounded.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The three-question close is highly actionable and one of the section’s best teaching devices.

#### §17.9 — four meanings of exponential speedup

- **Taxonomy value:** Separating gate-time, oracle-query, restricted-baseline, and input-access meanings is one of the
  book’s most practically valuable editorial choices.
- **Not quite four meanings:** Items 1–2 define a scaling/model distinction, item 3 changes the comparator, and item 4
  changes input access. They are independent axes and can overlap. A matrix with rows for query/time/output and columns
  for access/comparator would be more precise.
- **Ratio definition:** `$T_C/T_Q=2^{\Omega(n^c)}$` expresses an exponential-or-stretched-exponential ratio, but “best
  known classical” makes this an observed algorithmic gap, not a proved speedup/lower bound.
- **Shor wording:** Against GNFS, polynomial quantum factoring has a superpolynomial/subexponential-classical gap roughly
  of stretched-exponential form in bit length. “No quantum-resistant classical algorithm” is odd: the needed condition
  is simply no polynomial-time classical factoring algorithm (or no algorithm closing the claimed asymptotic gap).
- **Resource unit:** Counting abstract gates on both sides is not automatically comparable because classical gates,
  quantum logical gates, precision, and parallelism differ. For complexity classes, use time on standard models up to
  polynomial equivalence; for practice, use wall-clock/energy/cost.
- **Simon qualification:** Its query separation is unconditional within the specified promise-oracle model, exactly as
  stated.
- **Restricted simulation:** The tensor-network caveat is excellent, but tensor networks are only one alternative;
  sign-free Monte Carlo, stabilizer decompositions, and model-specific methods matter too.
- **HHL caricature:** Classical algorithms should be granted the closest operationally comparable sample/query access,
  not simply described as “denied” quantum access. Dequantisations match some, not all, block-encoding models and often
  pay different polynomial factors.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The four-way dissection punctures marketing language without losing the excitement of real
  separations.

#### §17.9 — practitioner rule

- **Excellent first question:** Requiring the intended model and comparator before accepting “exponential” should be a
  recurring boxed checklist.
- **“Bulletproof”:** Query lower bounds are rigorous under their oracle/promise/error conventions, but can be irrelevant
  to an explicit implementation. “Bulletproof within the model” is safer.
- **“Say little”:** Some query separations guide real algorithms and lower bounds substantially; the practical
  implication is conditional, not negligible.
- **Product claim:** Building a product requires finite-size crossover, useful output, reliability, capital/operating
  cost, verification, and a defensible classical baseline—not merely faith in absence of a matching asymptotic
  classical algorithm.
- **Restricted-model value:** Such results can identify representation bottlenecks and inspire methods even when they do
  not establish broad advantage.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Compact, skeptical, and immediately reusable.

#### §17.9 — input-loading rule

- **Core lesson:** Input preparation and output extraction belong in end-to-end complexity; this deserves the emphasis.
- **Example nuance:** An input may have `$2^n$` amplitudes but a succinct generator (product state, tensor network,
  arithmetic circuit, physical process). The relevant size is its shortest/available operational description, not the
  raw amplitude table.
- **Free-state input:** Some algorithms legitimately receive a quantum state produced by an upstream quantum process;
  charging classical loading would be equally unfair. State provenance defines the problem.
- **QRAM:** Separate one-time data-structure construction, coherent query time, update cost, hardware footprint, and
  fault-tolerant implementation. “Loading costs `$2^n$`” is not the only objection.
- **Cross-reference:** Verify Chapter 16 and Chapter 26 contain a real QRAM cost discussion and use consistent access-
  model terminology.
- **Output analogue:** Add the symmetric warning that tomography of an `$n$`-qubit answer can require exponentially many
  numbers/samples even if input loading is cheap.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** “The input has to come from somewhere” is a memorable, durable maxim.

#### §17.10 — query/time gap framing

- **Clear motivation:** The section correctly locates much of the theory-to-practice failure in uncounted oracle and
  surrounding costs.
- **Model distinction:** Query complexity ignores all computation between queries by design; time complexity must pick a
  machine/gate model, data representation, precision, and uniformity.
- **“Most algorithms”:** Some algorithms are intentionally oracle/subroutine results and remain valuable without a
  full application implementation. Avoid implying query research is merely unfinished time analysis.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Direct and operational, with little wasted prose.

#### §17.10 — three lift requirements

- **Oracle implementation:** Polynomial-size coherent implementation is necessary for a polynomial quantum upper bound,
  but it does not convert a classical *query lower bound* into an unconditional classical time lower bound for the
  explicit function. Classical algorithms may exploit the circuit’s internal structure without querying it as a
  black box.
- **Strong “lift cleanly” defect:** Even when all three listed conditions hold, a query separation does not
  automatically become a time separation. One also needs a time lower bound/reduction for the best unrestricted
  classical algorithm, plus efficient state preparation, non-query work, and output extraction.
- **Compilation:** “Efficient” should include target approximation error, synthesis overhead, connectivity/routing, and
  uniform compiler generation.
- **Fault-tolerance wording:** Error correction does not impose a constant overhead. Code distance and distillation
  overhead grow with circuit volume and target failure probability; asymptotic overhead may be polylogarithmic under a
  threshold theorem.
- **“Compatibility”:** Any universal fault-tolerant scheme can in principle compile a suitable circuit; the practical
  questions are logical gate set, geometry, feed-forward, non-Clifford factories, and overhead.
- **Shor caveat:** Shor has a polynomial quantum time algorithm because modular arithmetic and QFT are efficient, but
  its classical superpolynomial separation is still unproved.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The checklist is useful, but the claimed sufficiency is the section’s central technical error.

#### §17.10 — query-count ledger column

- **Definition:** Count oracle uses and separately count inverse, controlled, power, or precision-variable calls; they
  can have very different implementation costs.
- **Conceptual-equivalent ambiguity:** Data lookup, function evaluation, and time evolution are not interchangeable
  black boxes. Each needs an exact input/output unitary and cost convention.
- **Parameterized queries:** Hamiltonian simulation calls at time `$2^k t$` should not all count equally if cost scales
  with evolution time; use weighted queries when appropriate.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Functional and useful.

#### §17.10 — gate-count ledger column

- **Gate set:** Total count is meaningful only with a fixed logical/physical gate set and target approximation error.
- **Oracle inclusion:** Good that the bullet explicitly compiles the oracle; state whether measurements, resets,
  classical feed-forward, and magic-state preparation are counted.
- **Parallelism:** Gate count alone ignores opportunities and costs for concurrency; pair it with depth and spacetime
  volume.
- **Classical comparison:** Quantum gate count should not be compared directly to CPU/GPU operations without latency
  and architecture.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Clear but conventional.

#### §17.10 — depth ledger column

- **Definition:** Longest dependency chain is correct, but distinguish logical depth, T-depth, measurement depth, and
  hardware-scheduled depth after routing.
- **Coherence budget:** In fault-tolerant machines logical coherence is an error-budget/code-distance issue rather than
  raw physical coherence time; in NISQ circuits both matter.
- **Wall-clock:** Gate durations, measurement/feed-forward latency, factory throughput, and parallel hardware constrain
  the mapping from depth to time.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Necessary but could be made vivid with one contrasting circuit.

#### §17.10 — space ledger column

- **Logical/physical conflation:** Algorithmic qubits plus work ancillas give logical space. Error correction multiplies
  each logical qubit into many data/measurement qubits and adds routing/factory workspace; “including error-correction”
  needs a separate physical-qubit column.
- **Peak versus total:** Space should mean peak live qubits; spacetime volume captures qubit-time consumption.
- **Dirty/clean ancillas:** Availability and reset assumptions change compilation tradeoffs and should be recorded.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Useful bookkeeping, though the logical/physical merge would mislead estimates.

#### §17.10 — T-count and magic-state paragraph

- **T versus Toffoli defect:** A Toffoli is not one T gate. It may be decomposed into several T gates with ancilla/
  measurement tradeoffs or supplied through a CCZ/Toffoli magic state. Never write “T (or Toffoli) gates” as the same
  count.
- **Clifford-cost overstatement:** Clifford gates are cheaper in many codes, but not universally transversal and not
  free. In the surface code they require lattice surgery/braiding, measurements, routing, and code cycles.
- **Every-T wording:** Logical T gates are commonly implemented by magic-state injection/distillation in surface-code
  architectures, but alternatives include code switching, transversal gates in other codes, gauge fixing, and direct
  CCZ factories.
- **Factory dominance:** Often true for non-Clifford-heavy algorithms, not universal. Data block, routing, reaction
  latency, Clifford volume, or memory can dominate.
- **Strong synonym defect:** T-count is not synonymous with algorithm cost; T-depth/factory throughput, logical qubits,
  Clifford/measurement work, code distance, and target error determine spacetime volume.
- **Cross-reference:** Verify §8.10 does not repeat “transversal Clifford” or universal magic-state claims.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The factory picture is concrete, but its absolutes teach an architecture-specific heuristic as
  law.

#### §17.10 — RSA resource figures

- **Count conversion:** `$3\times10^9$` Toffolis cannot become a “corresponding T-count” without a chosen implementation.
  A direct CCZ-state resource estimate may not report T-count at all.
- **Primary-source requirement:** The 20-million-qubit/eight-hour estimate likely belongs to a specific Gidney–Ekerå
  architecture/noise/factory model; cite it and state logical error rate, physical error, cycle time, and success
  probability.
- **2025 refinement:** “Sub-million-qubit” is extremely current and must be verified from primary work, including
  runtime and changed assumptions. Lower qubits may trade for longer time or different hardware.
- **Cross-chapter consistency:** §15.3 must use identical dates, units, and distinction between logical/physical qubits.
- **False causal compression:** T/Toffoli count is one driver of factories, while arithmetic depth, memory, code
  distance, and architecture jointly produce qubit/runtime figures.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Concrete scale is irresistible and therefore demands unusually disciplined sourcing.

#### §17.10 — takeaway

- **Interest/practicality contrast:** Query complexity often reveals algorithmic novelty; practical cost needs a full
  resource ledger, not T-count alone.
- **Near-future mismatch:** Near-term non-fault-tolerant devices generally do not use logical T-count as the primary
  metric; native two-qubit depth, fidelity, connectivity, shots, and runtime matter. T-count targets fault-tolerant
  deployment.
- **Question revision:** Ask for logical T/CCZ count and depth, block-encoding/oracle circuit, logical qubits, physical
  spacetime estimate, and error budget.
- **Asymptotic value:** Do not demote query bounds: they can prove optimality and expose normalization/access bottlenecks
  even when finite hardware is distant.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Punchy and memorable, but its single-metric answer contradicts the ledger immediately above.

#### §17.11 — lower-bound framing

- **Strong scope overclaim:** Quantum *query complexity* has unusually mature lower-bound tools; quantum computational
  time/space complexity generally does not. Saying quantum complexity broadly is “one of the few areas” with routine
  lower bounds contradicts the section’s later admission.
- **Cross-reference:** Referring to §17.6 is efficient; this section should now focus on concrete consequences rather
  than repeat definitions.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The promise of limits is appealing, provided its scope is corrected at once.

#### §17.11 — polynomial-method examples

- **Search:** Polynomial approximate degree gives `$\Omega(\sqrt N)$`; sharp constants/optimality also have hybrid/
  adversary proofs.
- **Element distinctness:** Aaronson–Shi’s lower bound history and Ambainis’s quantum-walk upper bound should be cited
  precisely, including promise/input alphabet and whether log factors are absent.
- **Hamiltonian simulation:** Specify which lower bounds—no-fast-forwarding in time, precision dependence, sparse-oracle
  queries—and whether the polynomial method actually supplies each. “Bounds ... within polylog factors” is too vague.
- **Approximate counting:** Give a representative bound with error/relative-error and marked-fraction parameters.
- **Methods:** Do not imply all listed Hamiltonian lower bounds arise solely from the polynomial method if reductions or
  adversary/hybrid arguments are used.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Matching algorithmic exponents is satisfying; the last bullet needs enough detail to be
  meaningful.

#### §17.11 — adversary tightness

- **Capitalization:** Use “Boolean,” not “boolean.”
- **Scope:** The general/negative-weight adversary bound characterizes bounded-error quantum query complexity up to
  constants for finite functions under standard query models; total Boolean functions are a safe subset, but the text
  oscillates between “all” and “total.”
- **Equality notation:** “Equals ... up to constants” should be `$\Theta(\mathrm{Adv}^{\pm}(f))$`, not literal equality.
- **Attribution:** Reichardt’s span-program/tightness results have coauthors/extensions for general state conversion;
  cite the theorem matching this scope.
- **No-classical-analogue overclaim:** Classical randomized/deterministic decision-tree complexity has adversary,
  certificate, sensitivity, LP/minimax, and other characterizations. The general adversary theorem is unusually elegant,
  but “no universal characterisation” needs a precise comparator.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** A universal optimization formula for query complexity is genuinely striking.

#### §17.11 — open class-separation list

- **Status:** All five listed unrelativized relations are open, subject to the exact promise/language conventions.
- **Redundancy/dependence:** BQP⊄NP and NP⊄BQP together express conjectured incomparability; list them deliberately as
  two independent directions.
- **“Lower-bound statements”:** QMA≠QCMA and QMA(2)≠QMA are separation/equality questions, while proving one
  noncontainment is the needed lower bound. The phrasing is acceptable but could be sharper.
- **Evidence variation:** Oracle evidence is not uniform: QMA versus QCMA may use quantum oracles; identify classical
  versus quantum oracle strength. Candidate problems also differ greatly in naturalness.
- **Absence wording:** “Absence of contradicting algorithms” is weaker and odder than “failure to find simulations/
  inclusions”; an equality could hold without a known constructive algorithm at present.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The list is sobering and keeps the epistemic limits visible.

#### §17.11 — partition-function approximation

- **Formatting:** Replace inline code ``#P`` with math `$\#\mathrm P$`.
- **Problem scope:** Partition functions vary by spin model, graph family, signs/couplings, temperature, field, and
  requested relative/additive approximation. “#P-hard in general” is too coarse to guide practice.
- **FPRAS defect:** Planar zero-field Ising partition functions are often exactly polynomial-time computable via
  Pfaffian methods; “FPRAS under ... planar Ising” conflates distinct tractable regimes. Ferromagnetic Ising has an FPRAS
  much more broadly, while antiferromagnetic/high-temperature thresholds have model-specific classifications.
- **High temperature:** Correlation decay/mixing can yield approximation schemes, but thresholds depend on degree and
  interactions. Cite a precise theorem.
- **Quantum-walk claim:** Quadratic speedups apply to particular rapidly mixing Markov-chain/FPRAS constructions with
  coherent access, spectral-gap and state-preparation costs. They do not automatically accelerate every FPRAS.
- **No-algorithm overclaim:** “No polynomial-time algorithm” outside FPRAS regimes is not universally known; some
  regimes have other exact/approximate algorithms, and hardness is conditional/model-specific.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** This is a valuable application area, but the tractability map is presently too compressed to
  be correct.

#### §17.11 — translation-invariant ground energy

- **Base result:** Arbitrary local-Hamiltonian inverse-polynomial-gap energy decision is QMA-complete/hard as reviewed.
- **“Exact” concern:** Gottesman–Irani’s 1D translationally invariant result uses succinctly encoded system size and a
  promise gap suited to QMA_EXP; it is not simply exact real-number ground-energy computation. State the finite-size,
  local-dimension, boundary, encoding, and precision conditions.
- **Hard versus complete:** Verify whether the cited formulation is QMA_EXP-complete or merely hard.
- **Why elevated class:** When chain length is exponential in the binary input length, an exponential-time verifier in
  the succinct description length becomes natural. Explain this; otherwise QMA_EXP appears from nowhere.
- **Notation:** Define `$\mathrm{QMA}_{\mathrm{EXP}}$` before using it.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Translation invariance failing to remove worst-case hardness is a powerful surprise.

#### §17.11 — spectral-gap undecidability

- **Thermodynamic limit essential:** The Cubitt–Pérez-García–Wolf theorem concerns families of translationally invariant
  two-dimensional Hamiltonians in the infinite-size/thermodynamic-limit setting, not a finite Hamiltonian matrix.
- **Input/model:** State fixed local dimension status, interaction specification, spectral-gap definition uniform in
  system size, and which later refinements alter dimensions/symmetries.
- **“Some settings”:** Correctly cautious, but “non-trivial class” should not imply typical laboratory Hamiltonians
  inherit undecidability.
- **Uncomputability versus inefficiency:** Undecidable is stronger than “no efficient algorithm” and unconditional for
  the theorem’s formal family. Keep it separate from QMA-hardness under standard conjectures.
- **Citation:** Verify the 2015 publication/preprint details and accents/hyphenation.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The leap from computational hardness to undecidability is one of the chapter’s strongest
  moments.

#### §17.11 — negative-results conclusion

- **Logical mixture:** QMA-hardness alone does not say no efficient algorithm unless BQP≠QMA (or P/NP-style assumptions);
  #P approximation hardness depends on reductions/complexity collapse assumptions; spectral-gap undecidability is
  unconditional. Do not summarize all three as merely “under standard conjectures.”
- **Heuristics:** Hard worst cases explain why unrestricted guarantees fail, but heuristic/special-case algorithms can
  be efficient and certifiable on structured instances.
- **Map metaphor:** Excellent: lower bounds mark where assumptions, restrictions, or heuristics must enter.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The ending is constructive rather than defeatist, a good tonal choice.

#### §17.12 — dequantisation definition

- **Definition scope:** A dequantisation is a classical algorithm reproducing the relevant output/guarantee of a quantum
  algorithm with comparable access and complexity, often up to polynomial losses. It need not have comparable runtime
  in every parameter or be quantum-inspired.
- **Spelling:** British “dequantised” is consistent within the section; establish project-wide convention against
  American “qubitization.”
- **Comparable versus same access:** Classical sample/query access and coherent quantum state/oracle access are rarely
  literally identical operations. Define what preprocessing, updates, norm queries, precision, and sampling each model
  supplies.
- **Output/error:** Matching runtime is meaningless unless both algorithms solve the same sampling/estimation task to
  the same error and success probability.
- **Conceptual scaffolding:** Quantum-inspired algorithms often borrow randomized linear algebra ideas as well as
  quantum structure; avoid implying a single derivation pattern.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The idea is introduced cleanly and carries immediate critical force.

#### §17.12 — Kerenidis–Prakash setup

- **Algorithm characterization:** Verify whether the recommendation algorithm is accurately called “HHL-based”; it uses
  quantum linear-algebra/singular-value estimation machinery, but the exact primitive and output matter.
- **Complexity incomplete:** `$O(\mathrm{poly}(\log N,\kappa,1/\epsilon))$` hides rank, recommendation-quality,
  sampling, data-structure construction, sparsity/norm, and success parameters. Quote the actual theorem.
- **Dimension:** Define `$N$` and whether the matrix is `$m\times n$`; one symbol may conceal user/product dimensions.
- **Classical baseline:** “Exponentially faster” was conditional on a particular data-access/preprocessing comparison,
  not a proved lower bound against all classical algorithms. Say “claimed/appeared exponentially faster in dimension.”
- **Citation/date:** Verify 2016 preprint/publication and the exact input data structure assumed.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Recommendation systems provide a relatable setting for an abstract access-model reversal.

#### §17.12 — Tang’s access observation and result

- **Access model precision:** Tang-style sample-and-query access usually permits querying entries, sampling row/column
  indices proportional to squared magnitude, and querying/navigating norm data. “Sampling access ... with known norms”
  needs a formal oracle/data-structure definition.
- **Same-access nuance:** Quantum state preparation may require a tree/QRAM-like data structure from which a classical
  sampler can derive SQ access, but coherent access and classical samples are not identical capabilities.
- **Runtime comparison:** “Matched ... up to polynomial factors” can preserve polylogarithmic dimension dependence while
  being dramatically worse in rank, condition number, and inverse error. Report the complete exponents and practical
  crossover.
- **“Speedup disappeared”:** The claimed exponential dependence on dimension was removed under the matched structured-
